# agent_persona: a Fable-tier coordinator directing many Sonnet workers across repos, v2

Status: In Progress
Commit Model: Branch-and-PR. `main` is protected on GitHub, PRs only. Every PR opens Draft and the worker marks it ready explicitly, in words, per v1's own convention.
Created: 2026-09-12

## Dispatch Authorization

The operator authorized a self-armed completion leash over this plan on 2026-09-13, over the Discord thread bound to the dev session, covering any session holding this plan. The authorization answered the dev session's recommendation to arm the plan so a stop cannot end the run between sections. It grants the leash and nothing wider: outward acts outside the recorded commit model still need the operator's yes.

## Section 0. Close out v1 first, plus two live worker-liveness defects a fleet cannot ship with

`agent_persona_passive-supervisor_v1.md` is still Status: In Progress; every roadmap item (1-8) has a Chapter, everything is merged to `main` at `1911a83`, and this v2 spec exists at that same clean boundary. Before any v2 section starts:

1. **The goal-less-restart defect (Reviewer Round 105 R1, memory: `a-goal-less-worker-restarts-on-every-operator-message`).** The item 2 backstop (`hooks/index.ts:2748`) backfills a one-round root and marks it complete in the same tick for *any* owner turn that did real tool work with no active root - whatever the turn's origin, coordinator-delivered included. `bin/supervise.sh` reads that `root_complete` as the goal finishing and takes `RESTART_PASSIVE`, killing a live child that was never actually done with anything; live on the `aios` worker three times in one afternoon (16:04Z, 16:10Z, 16:25:35Z). Fix: the supervisor's restart decision ignores a root marked as backfilled. The wiring this needs is more than a trigger-read tweak (Reviewer Round 109 F5): `bin/supervise-decide.mjs` only ever receives a bare timestamp, `rootCompleteTs`, read by `get_fact` (`bin/supervise.sh:227-247`), which filters `decisions[]` by `action` and prints the newest match's `timestamp` - it never reads a goal node's `notes[]`, where the backfill marker actually lives (`hooks/index.ts:2763`), and never reads a decision's own `detail` text either. The backfill's own `root_complete` decision already writes `"...marked complete - backfilled, work already done"` in its `detail` (`hooks/index.ts:2777-2783`), so the fix is: a new sibling function beside `get_fact` (Reviewer Round 113 R39 - `get_fact` itself stays single-token and unchanged, since its existing bare-timestamp callers are pure numeric comparisons that a second word would break) reads the same decision and also checks whether its `detail` contains the literal substring `"backfilled"`, emitting `<timestamp> backfilled` vs `<timestamp> real`; `bin/supervise-decide.mjs`'s restart trigger treats a `backfilled` `root_complete` as "no restart, this child is still working" rather than a completion signal.
   - **Two readers of `root_complete`, not one (Reviewer Round 111 R22).** The decide-unit path (`bin/supervise-decide.mjs`) is not the only place that acts on this fact: `bin/supervise.sh`'s own natural-exit path (`:686-688`) reads `get_fact ... root_complete` directly in bash and takes `RESTART_PASSIVE` with no decide-unit involved at all - this is the exact path that fired three times on `aios` today, not the decide-unit path. The fix applies to both: the natural-exit read gets the same "ignore a backfilled root" logic, sourced from the same second token `get_fact` now emits. That second token is additive, not a replacement: `get_fact`'s existing single-token output (used elsewhere for plain timestamp comparisons, e.g. the `-gt` checks at `bin/supervise.sh:676, 681, 687`) is unchanged, and the backfill-aware read is a new sibling function so those existing bash numeric comparisons never receive a two-word string.
   - **Test (Reviewer Round 109 R9, extended per R22):** `.kit/supervisor-unit-test.mjs` (which imports only `bin/supervise-decide.mjs`, per Reviewer Round 113 R39, and so proves only the decide-unit path) gains a case that a backfilled `root_complete` (newer than child start) yields no `RESTART_PASSIVE`, and a control that a real, non-backfilled completed root still restarts as it does today. The bash natural-exit path (`bin/supervise.sh:686-691`) is a genuinely separate code path that unit test cannot reach; its own coverage is `.kit/live-restartpassive-test.sh` (the existing shape for a live end-to-end proof of this file), extended with the same backfilled-vs-real distinction.
   - **Landing plan:** this is a v1 fix (the defect lives entirely in v1's own shipped code), so it lands under `agent_persona_passive-supervisor_v1.md`'s own Branch-and-PR model, with its own Chapter, before v2's Section 1 starts - not folded into a v2 PR.
2. **The stop-path defect (Reviewer Round 105 R2, same memory record).** A `RESTART_PASSIVE`/`stop_child` cycle can TERM the bash wrapper while `claude.exe` survives underneath it, still holding the persona claim; the 120-second pre-gate then times out and `supervise.sh` exits 2, leaving an orphaned child and no supervisor watching it. Correction to the first draft (Reviewer Round 109 F9): `bin/supervise.sh` does **not** already record a PID lineage anywhere on disk - `CHILD_PID` is a plain shell variable (`:159, :328`) holding the one `claude` process's own PID, no file, no descendant tracking. Fix: `stop_child` (`bin/supervise.sh:177-215`) captures `$pid`'s descendant process tree at stop time and sends TERM/KILL to every process in it, not just `$pid` itself - on this Windows environment, a `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` parent-PID walk is the actual mechanism (Reviewer Round 111 R29 correction: `.kit/live-commons-test.sh:56`'s `Get-Process -Id` is a liveness check only, not a descendant walk, and no such walk exists anywhere in this codebase to point at as precedent - this is new code). **One more translation this walk needs (Reviewer Round 113 R42):** `CHILD_PID` (`bin/supervise.sh:159, 328, 435`, checked with `kill -0`) is the coproc's MSYS pid, not a Windows pid - `Win32_Process` keys on the latter. `$pid` is translated via `ps -W` (or `/proc/$pid/winpid`) to its Windows pid before the `Win32_Process` walk starts; walking from the untranslated MSYS pid would silently match nothing. The pre-gate's retry ceiling is already an explicit number (`120`, `bin/supervise.sh:304`, into `wait_persona_free_both`); this section's own correction is only that the ceiling now applies to a real process-tree stop rather than a single-PID one, with a log line on each retry.
   - **Test (Reviewer Round 109 R9):** a live case - a child whose `claude` process outlives its own bash wrapper (simulated by detaching it before calling `stop_child`) is still fully stopped, the persona claim frees, and the pre-gate passes within its stated retry ceiling. **Ruling (Reviewer Round 126 R76, worded per R93):** the claim-free and pre-gate-pass observables are covered, for the EOF stop path, by `.kit/live-restartpassive-test.sh` F2. `.kit/live-stopprocesstree-test.sh` itself has no EOF-only case; its own two cases reach TERM and KILL respectively. The gap this ruling accepts is specifically the KILL-path shape (a child that ignores both EOF and TERM, forcing `stop_child` to its final escalation) proving those same two observables - backlogged rather than blocking this item's PR.
   - **Landing plan:** also a v1 fix, same reasoning as item 1 - its own PR against v1, its own Chapter, before v2's Section 1.
3. **Worker launch discipline (operator-directed, Round 136 addendum).** Evidence: this session's own transcript shows every assistant turn on `claude-sonnet-5`, exactly two Skill invocations across the whole session (`brainstorming`, `workflow-authoring`), and no Agent or Workflow dispatch after 21:16Z across five PR #17 commits; the `aios` worker's Chapter 21 records "review rounds 0, no fresh-context review dispatched". Neither worker ever loaded `operating-instructions` or `executing-work`, so the per-section reviewer pair, the fix-round loop, and the red-before-green rule reached the worker only as summarized doctrine, not as a loaded, followed procedure. This is the root cause behind Round 132's R100 and Round 134's R104: both Chapter sentences claimed a fix that was not in the diff, exactly the shape a fresh-context blind reviewer on the diff catches every time and a same-context worker does not.
   - **Part A (skill loading on the priming turn).** Site: the priming write beside `CHANNEL_REPLY_INSTRUCTION` (`bin/supervise.sh:393` on `main`); Section 3 item 1 already plans a standing instruction there that fires regardless of `NO_CHANNEL`, so this rides the same `NO_CHANNEL`-independent write rather than a second one. The sentence, verbatim intent: "Before your first tool call on any plan work, invoke the Skill tool for `claude-kit:operating-instructions`, then `claude-kit:executing-work`; when a plan reaches its last section, `claude-kit:finishing-work`. After any context compaction, re-invoke the governing skill before the next step, because compaction drops skill bodies. A fix round inside a review loop is a section: it takes the same fresh-context adversarial and blind reviewer pair before you post it, and the round cites their verdicts beside the gate count."
   - **Test (Part A):** extend the pattern of `.kit/channel-reply-instruction-test.sh` to assert the priming turn carries the skill-load sentence under both `NO_CHANNEL` values; a live leg reads the child's `stdout.jsonl` for a `Skill` `tool_use` naming `operating-instructions` within the first three assistant turns.
   - **Part B (model tier, decided).** Operator's decision, recorded under DISCUSSION.md's Round 136 addendum: `supervisorModel` defaults to `opus`, the worker's main thread runs at medium effort, and per-section implementer tier is unaffected - a dispatched section still takes whatever tier the plan doc's own `Model:` line names, under executing-work. Site: `--model "${MODEL:-haiku}"` at `bin/supervise.sh:409` (pre-fix line), and the launchers that export `MODEL` (e.g. `/d/personas/aios/relaunch-wait.sh:12` sets `sonnet`). Change: `SUPERVISOR_MODEL` and `SUPERVISOR_EFFORT` settings read beside `SUPERVISOR_POLL_MS`, fed into the launch call's `--model` and `--effort` flags; an explicit `MODEL`/`EFFORT` env var still overrides either - the `.kit/live-*` suites that launch `bin/supervise.sh` each `export MODEL="haiku"` for exactly this reason, so their own cost is unaffected by the new default (Reviewer Round 141 R109: this export must reach the launched child's real environment, not just be set as a plain shell variable in the suite's own script, since the suite launches the supervisor as a separate process). **The persona launchers' own `MODEL=sonnet` export is not dropped by this item's own PR**: `/d/personas/aios/relaunch-wait.sh` launches a *different, stale* runtime clone (`/d/DeepSeekHarness/agentic-plugin`, confirmed at `1911a83` as of this writing) that does not carry this fix yet - dropping that export before the clone is updated would silently downgrade that worker to the old bare `haiku` default, not up to `opus`. Left as a named follow-up for whoever next updates that runtime clone, not silently completed as if already safe.
   - **Test (Part B):** the unit test asserts the launch line's model comes from the setting (with whichever default the operator picks); the `.kit/live-*` suites continue to pass `haiku` explicitly so their own cost is unaffected by the new default.
   - **Part C (the record).** This item's own Chapter names the two workers' transcripts as the evidence and the doctrine line "a reviewer pair on every section" as the rule it makes mechanical, rather than restating the incident narrative. A `docs/backlog.md` entry captures the kit-side lesson (the `executing-work` skill should say in words that a fix round inside a review loop takes the pair) - that entry belongs to the kit repo, not this one, and is a pointer, not a fix landed here.
4. Whole-gate live run of `.kit/live-all.sh` at the tip carrying fixes 1, 2, and 3 (hooks changed since the last whole gate at `661759c`: `742015b` the channel-reply backstop, `adfffcc` the dedupe fix, plus this section's own three), pasted verbatim into a closing Chapter. Run only once the operator has confirmed the machine is otherwise quiet (every other live `claude` process stopped) - Section 5's extended lock does not exist yet at this point in the sequence, since it is itself v2 code the whole gate is validating; this section cannot depend on it. **This item runs before item 5's installed-mode switch (Reviewer Round 113 R43), so its own Chapter evidence covers only the `--plugin-dir` (dev-tree) load** - every v2 session goes on to load the installed copy instead, a surface this gate never exercises. Item 5's own live check is the acceptance for the installed-mode load; this item's whole-gate evidence is not read as covering it.
5. Item 6's own unfinished half: the runtime switch from `D:\DeepSeekHarness\agentic-plugin` (`--plugin-dir`) to the installed marketplace plugin as this machine's one shared plugin copy - load-bearing for v2, since every worker and the coordinator must load the same plugin code for the commons store to mean the same thing everywhere (memory: `a-second-worker-launches-in-its-own-repo-with-a-unique-persona-name`). Acceptance includes a live check that the installed-mode store actually holds every currently-live persona claim (worker and coordinator alike) before any other section trusts it as the one shared store.
6. Finishing reviews (adversarial + docs-curator) per the finishing-work skill.

**Not done as of this spec's authoring:** this machine currently runs 4+ live `claude` processes (this worker, the Reviewer, the `aios` worker, at minimum) at 345-597 MB each. The whole-gate run takes about 25 minutes and launches many more short-lived children that all contend for the one shared commons-store file. Running it blind against that contention risks exactly the collision failures v1's own Chapters (1, 2, 7) already hit from concurrent live sessions, and items 1-2 above are net-new fixes this spec has not yet built, let alone proven. This section is deliberately not executed inline in this round. Operating rule for when it does run (Reviewer Round 105 R4): stop every worker, run the gate, relaunch the fleet - with `dev` and `aios` running all day, the gate does not get a naturally quiet window on its own.

## Goal

Today, a "worker" is a `bin/supervise.sh` launch: one Sonnet-tier `claude -p` child, one repo, one persona, one Discord thread. The operator drives each one directly. v2 adds one Fable-tier "coordinator" persona that reads status across N workers in N repos, batches steers to them without blocking on their multi-minute turns, receives their findings and escalations, reports to the operator on its own thread, and - per the Decisions section's FORK B ruling - dispatches bounded work to a worker without a human round trip per message, within a checkable bound and a judgment call the operator has explicitly delegated. The mechanism is an extension of the existing commons-store inbox path (`agentic_say`/`agentic_inbox`), not a new transport. Discord stays the operator's channel; the coordinator does not read or post into a worker's thread.

## Approach

**Grounding.** This design was pressure-tested by a two-lens Fable-tier design council (Round 1, 2026-09-12) before being written down: an operability/reliability lens and an authority/safety lens, converged by a facilitator. Every claim about the plugin's own code below was independently confirmed against `hooks/index.ts`, `hooks/operator.ts`, `hooks/commons.ts`, and `bin/supervise.sh`, both by the council and, for the load-bearing ones, spot-checked again by this session before this line was written. The full council transcript is retained at `.kit/runs/v2-council-round1/` (copied out of the workflow's own transcript dir before archiving, per the memory note `a-run-dir-is-merge-evidence-until-the-merge-lands`).

**The rejected mechanisms, and why.** Two other transports exist on this machine and both were considered and rejected as the coordinator-to-worker channel:
- The harness's own peer tools (`ListAgents`/`SendMessage`): this plugin actively consumes `session.receive` events of kind `peer`/`peer-send-message` before the model ever sees them (`hooks/index.ts:694-712`), specifically so a peer message carries no steering authority. Using it as a coordinator channel means reversing that design decision and rebuilding deferred-status, urgent break-in, reply capture, and the reader-claim trust gate on a transport this plugin does not own. Its address is a live session id, which a `supervise.sh` relaunch mints fresh every time (Chapter 8.3's own proof: child-1, then child-2), while `persona:<name>` is the thing that actually stays stable across a restart.
- Each worker's own Discord thread: inbound authority there is the operator's own Discord account against a one-account allowlist; the relay's own instructions tell a worker to treat channel text as keyboard-equivalent operator steering. A coordinator posting there either launders its own text into operator authority with no way for the worker to tell the difference, or has no channel to post through at all, since the relay never tells one process which session serves another process's thread. Read access is closed the same way. Discord stays the operator's channel, full stop; the coordinator reports to the operator on its own thread, same as any worker does today.
- `DISCUSSION.md` (the append-only file the worker and Reviewer personas use today for handoffs): turn-serial, invisible to the plugin's own trust gate, and polled by hand. It works between two co-equal live sessions watching one repo; it does not scale to a coordinator directing N workers in N repos, none of which read it. v2 retires it as a steering path; the plan doc and the coordinator's own thread are the record going forward.

**The chosen mechanism: the commons-store inbox path, extended in four small places.** `agentic_say`/`agentic_inbox` already has everything this needs except cross-repo reach: a machine-checked writer gate (only a live reader-claim holder's record reaches the model, `hooks/index.ts:3736-3740`), a deferred-status view that doesn't block on a busy worker (`:3784-3808`), an urgent break-in that reaches a worker mid-turn through the `tool.call` hook's own `context` return (`:3830-3868`), reply capture, and a decision-log trail. Item 8.3 built these for the operator-to-worker direction; the coordinator reuses them rather than inventing a second version.

Four extensions, in order of what blocks the next:
1. **Cross-repo deferred status.** `heartbeatPath` is the literal string `".agentic-heartbeat.json"`, read relative to the process's own cwd (`hooks/index.ts:408, 425, 581`). A coordinator running in one repo can never see a worker in another repo as busy; it would read its own empty or irrelevant heartbeat file. Fix: publish `turnStartedAt` and `workdir` into the session's commons entry (`hooks/commons.ts:24-28`, keyed `commons:<sessionId>`, one entry per live session, already a machine-global store). Finding the right entry for a named persona is `commonsWinner(readAllClaims(...), "persona:<target>")` (`hooks/commons.ts`), the same lookup `shouldYieldCommons` already uses for arbitration - not a lookup this design invents.
2. **Addressing N workers.** `agentic_say`/`agentic_inbox` operate on `sess.persona` only. Reaching a specific worker today means an `agentic_identity` switch, which also swaps the coordinator's own in-memory state to that persona's store, losing its own. Fix: an optional `persona` argument on both tools, so the coordinator addresses a worker by name without switching identity.
3. **Coordinator-to-worker and worker-to-coordinator addressing, both authorized without a reader claim - because a reader claim cannot cover this shape at all.** (Correction, Reviewer Round 111 R25: worker-to-coordinator addressing needs the caller to own *some* persona, per Section 3's own text - a reader-only session with no owned persona, this Reviewer's own shape, is refused when addressing the coordinator. "No special claim at all" below means no claim *beyond ordinary ownership*, not no requirement whatsoever.) `claimReaderRole` (`hooks/index.ts:1036, 1138, 3170`, also reached at session start on lost commons arbitration) is only ever reached through `agentic_identity` switching `sess.persona`, which also releases `persona:<previous>` (`hooks/index.ts:3112`) - so a session that owns `persona:coordinator` cannot also hold `reader:<worker>` for N workers without giving up its own persona each time it addresses one. (A *reader-only* coordinator, with no persona of its own, is the live counterexample - the Reviewer session holds `reader:dev`, `reader:reviewer`, and `reader:aios` simultaneously today, and this is a real, working alternative shape. It is rejected here because the coordinator needs its own supervisor lifecycle - restarts, a goal tree, a Discord thread of its own - which only an owned persona gets; a reader has none of that. So the choice is "the coordinator owns a persona, like a worker does" plus a new authorization rule for reaching *other* personas from that position, not an appeal to impossibility.) The new rule: the caller must hold `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` (the one configured coordinator name, `cfg.coordinatorPersona`, read beside `controllerTickMs` at `hooks/index.ts:648`) to address any *other* persona, **or** the target persona must equal `<coordinatorPersona>` (so any worker can push to the coordinator without holding any special claim at all - the coordinator's own name is the address, known config rather than a credential the worker must acquire). Both `agentic_say`'s owner-only denial (`hooks/index.ts:3731-3733`) and `agentic_inbox`'s matching owner-only denial (`:3768-3771`) are relaxed by exactly this rule: refused only when the target equals the caller's own persona (self-messaging stays blocked, for everyone, no exception); allowed for any other target when either side of the "or" holds. No new reader-claim path, no new tool.

   This alone is not sufficient for a record to actually reach a worker's model: `hasLiveReaderClaim` (`hooks/operator.ts:407-417`) matches only a `reader:<persona>` resource and has no notion of a coordinator or owner claim at all, so it gates the send (`hooks/index.ts:3736-3740`), the matching `agentic_inbox` check (`:3773-3777`), the delivery-tick filter (`:1336`, marks `"skipped"` at `:1345`), the urgent path (`:3846`), and the ask-answer delivery (`:1266`) - five places, not the three the first cut of this design named, that would silently swallow a coordinator's own record or a worker's push to the coordinator. Section 3 composes a new check at all five rather than assuming the existing gate already covers this shape. Without this, Section 4's labels are computed for records that never arrive.
4. **Provenance label.** Every delivered record today is submitted to the model as a flat `[OPERATOR] <text>` or `[OPERATOR, urgent] <text>` (`hooks/index.ts:1372, 3860`), with no distinction between the real operator (who no longer even uses this path since Discord attaches directly) and any reader session, coordinator included. Fix: label a record `[COORDINATOR] <text>` when the writer's session holds `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` in commons, computed at delivery time from the writer's live claims (not stored on the record at send, so a claim that changed hands between send and delivery is read as it stands at delivery - the same freshness `hasLiveReaderClaim` already uses elsewhere), and reserve `[OPERATOR]` for records that actually originate from the operator's own Discord channel path (untouched by this section - it never goes through this delivery code). A non-coordinator reader (the Reviewer, the relay) gets a distinct `[READER:<persona>]` label rather than either. This is a prerequisite for the FORK B decision below, not optional plumbing: without it, a worker cannot apply any coordinator-specific rule to coordinator text, because it cannot tell coordinator text from anyone else's. Round 106 is the live argument for why this matters now rather than later: the item 8.3 urgent break-in delivered a Reviewer steer into a *subagent's* tool-call result labelled `[OPERATOR, urgent]`, the subagent (correctly, per doctrine) refused to act on unverifiable injected text, and the worker reported to the operator that something on the machine could inject fake authoritative-looking messages - which was true, and is exactly the provenance gap this label closes. Section 4 below adds the further fix Round 106 asks for: the urgent break-in must never fire inside a dispatched subagent's own tool call, only the top-level session's.

**Machine-budget contention.** Confirmed live via `Get-Process` at council time: 4+ `claude` processes at 345-597 MB each, plus their node children. A coordinator adds one more process pair; each worker adds one more. The mechanism choice does not change this count. Two contention findings, both grounded in the live store rather than guessed: `listInboxRecords` and `hasLiveReaderClaim` each poll the one shared JSON file every tick per owner plus on a 5-second throttle per tool call, so K workers plus one coordinator is K+1 pollers on one file; and the store shows two separate clusters of multiple full-file `.bak` rewrites within a third of a second of each other, consistent with concurrent processes each writing a full snapshot in quick succession (confirmed as contention; not confirmed as corruption - all copies parsed as valid JSON with matching key counts). Mitigations, detailed in Section 5: the coordinator's own launch sets its tick cadence well above the worker default (a launch-time setting, no new knob); the existing closed-record roll to the append-only log stays in place; and `.kit/live-all.sh` gains a new refuse-at-start check - the whole gate does not run at all while any real fleet is live, not merely waits for it, per Section 5.

**Operating rule that falls out of the turn-serial constraint.** A worker's own turn runs 2-5 minutes; a message delivered mid-turn queues until it ends (confirmed live in this plan's own Rounds 100-103: a message sent to this worker mid-turn queued exactly this way). So: steers to one worker batch into one record per cycle rather than trickling in one at a time; `urgent` is reserved for a real stop-or-redirect, never for "what is your status"; and status is never asked of a worker in-band - the coordinator reads `agentic_inbox`, the worker's own `.agentic-personas.json` in its repo, and the commons entry, all of which are file reads with no turn cost to the worker.

## Standing Brief Amendments

- Section 0 item 5's scope is which plugin copy a worker or the coordinator loads, how the settings a supervisor writes reach that copy, and whether the installed-mode store holds every live persona claim. How a supervisor's cadence values resolve between exported env vars and the library's `PROFILE` block is a different surface and does not ride under item 5.
- Completing a provided settings file under item 5 means copying the options from whichever plugin id carries them to the id that lacks them, with every option as the caller wrote it. Which persona wins when a supervisor's persona argument and a provided settings file disagree is a different surface, present identically under `--plugin-dir`, and does not ride under item 5.
- A worker whose child exits 0 with a backfilled root_complete newer than its start is relaunched without RESTART_PASSIVE and outside the crash counter. That is what item 1's "ignores a backfilled root" means on the bash natural-exit path.
- Bash unsets a coproc's `CHILD_PID` when it reaps the child, and `bin/supervise.sh` runs under `set -u`, so any read of `CHILD_PID` after the child exits aborts the supervisor with exit 1. Every read of the child's pid after launch uses one pid saved immediately after the coproc starts. The RESTART_PASSIVE restarts on `aios` came through the decide path's stop, not the natural-exit branch, which no run had reached.
- The bash natural-exit path's coverage for item 1 is `.kit/supervisor-natural-exit-test.sh`, a stub-driven suite, in place of the live-restartpassive legs the Test bullet names. A live suite cannot reach that branch, because its child blocks on stdin until cleanup stops it.
- Every numeric supervisor setting passes one shared check: digits only, no leading zero, at most 9 digits, greater than zero. Bash reads a leading zero as octal in arithmetic, and a value past 64 bits breaks `[ -lt ]`. A setting the code consumes as `$((value / 1000))` is checked in the unit its consumer uses, so a sub-second value cannot floor to a zero wait.
- `staleAfterMs` reaches a `node` program through argv rather than spliced into the program body, and it passes the numeric check on the provided-settings path, which skips `emit_settings_json`.
- Both node programs inside `wait_persona_free_both` take the stale bound through argv, so the commons check and the heartbeat check read the same value.
- Section 0 repairs the two liveness defects its title names and the surfaces its six items build. A supervisor behavior that predates Section 0 and that none of those items changes, such as when a restart limit fires or how long a launch prompt is kept, is a different surface and does not ride under Section 0.
- A test lands only where it pins a failure the code can actually produce and a hard requirement depends on. A test that pins a setting's value, a preference, or a configuration choice does not land. A case that another case or control already proves is removed rather than added.
- Section 13 runs after Section 0 closes and before Section 1.
- A live suite is owed for an engine contract only where that contract has already changed under the plugin once. A contract shape `tsc` reads from `.claude/types` is pinned by the typecheck, and the offline harness carries the logic behind it.
- Section 6's launch shape never feeds a worker's `--prompt` from coordinator text. `bin/supervise.sh` frames `--prompt` content as the operator's trusted task, so coordinator steers reach a worker only through the labelled inbox path.
- Section 12 runs after Sections 1 and 2 and before Section 3. Sections 7 and 8 each carry one added sentence: a worker resolves a coordinator record with `agentic_resolve` when the work is finished or declined, and the coordinator counts rounds per steer against resolutions rather than replies.
- Section 4's delivery label carries the record id at both tick delivery sites and the ask-answer delivery, as `[COORDINATOR id=<record id>]`, `[READER:<persona> id=<record id>]` and `[WORKER:<persona> id=<record id>]`, so a worker holds the id `agentic_resolve` needs; nothing else in-band tells the owner a record's id, since `agentic_inbox` is reader-only. Section 7's standing instruction names where the id sits in the prompt.
- A delivery site whose `$.prompt.submit` rejects consumes the stamp handoff it armed and records the refusal in the decision log, and nothing else: the record stays as the delivery wrote it and ages out under the TTL, the ask it answered and the goal node it activated stay as the delivery left them, and no delivery is retried.
- A delivery site whose `$.prompt.submit` resolves with `{ drop }` takes the same path as one whose submit rejects, with the drop reason as the recorded refusal: the stamp handoff is consumed, the record stays as the delivery wrote it, nothing is retried. The other plugin-submitted turns (nudge, kaizen, backstop, re-raise) consume their own handoff on a drop the same way. A delivery entry also leaves the list when the withheld branch finds its record absent, or no longer delivered and unstamped, which it reads from the store once per fire before naming the first surviving entry. The list carries no bound of its own beyond those consumptions.
- Decided 2026-09-14 by the operator on the dev session's Discord thread: FORK B's four ask-first items are struck. A coordinator steer inside a worker's approved plan acts on a push, a deploy, a settings or CLAUDE.md edit and a change to the plan's commit model on its own delegated authority; the repository's branch protection and the pull-request review are the controls, and the plugin builds no internal block on any of them. Section 7 item 2 and FORK B's revision paragraph carry the rule.
- Decided 2026-09-14 by the operator on the supervisor's Discord thread: a worker's send to the coordinator persona is accepted while no live session owns that persona, and the record waits `pending` until a coordinator's first tick, the form Section 3's delivery test and Section 12 acceptance 3 already state. The plugin refuses no send on the ground that the coordinator is unheld, and the worker's standing instruction carries no retry clause for one. The operator's ground: the supervisor loop is to become self-healing and restart with the machine, so a record that waits for the coordinator to come back is the intended shape.

## Sections of Work

### 1. Commons entry carries turn state and workdir
Model: opus
`hooks/commons.ts`'s per-session commons entry gains `turnStartedAt: number | null` and `workdir: string`. **Correction (Reviewer Round 111 R24 - this line was reported fixed in Round 110 and was not):** the commons entry is not written at turn start or turn complete today, only at `claimResource`/`releaseResource` call sites (`hooks/index.ts:1072, 1128, 3131`) - `turn.start` (`:2592-2600`) and `turn.complete` (`:2674-2676`) write the *heartbeat file* only. This section adds a commons-entry write at both of those two hooks, beside the existing `writeOwnerHeartbeat` calls there, so the new fields track a turn's lifecycle the same way the heartbeat file's own `turnStartedAt` already does. No existing reader of the commons entry breaks: these are additive fields.
Files in scope: `hooks/commons.ts`, `hooks/index.ts` (every commons-entry write site), `hooks/operator.ts` (the reader-role claim and release, which are commons-entry write sites; folded in at review round 2).
Tests: a harness case that a commons entry written mid-turn (at the `turn.start` handler) carries a non-null `turnStartedAt` and the entry's own `workdir`, and a control that `turn.complete` (`hooks/index.ts:2674-2676`, the same site that already clears the heartbeat's own turn stamp) clears the commons entry's copy back to null too.

### 2. Deferred status reads the commons entry, not the local heartbeat file
Model: opus
`agentic_inbox`'s deferred-status check (`hooks/index.ts:3784-3808`) reads the target persona's commons entry (Section 1's new fields) instead of the cwd-relative `.agentic-heartbeat.json`, so a coordinator in one repo correctly reads a worker in another repo as busy or free.
Files in scope: `hooks/commons.ts` (the holder-meta reader), `hooks/index.ts`, `README.md:369` (the `agentic_inbox` paragraph, which described the heartbeat-file deferred read; the spec's `:285` had drifted; Reviewer Round 111 R30 - updated to describe the commons-entry read instead).
Tests: a harness case with two simulated working directories/personas proving the deferred check reads the addressed persona's own commons state, not the caller's local heartbeat file; a control proving the same-repo case (today's only working case) still passes.

### 3. `persona` argument on `agentic_say` / `agentic_inbox`, authorized without a reader claim
Model: fable
Both tools take an optional `persona` argument; when given, the write/read targets that persona's inbox rather than `sess.persona`, with no identity switch.

**The self-message guard (Reviewer Round 109 F2 correction).** The naive reading - "denied when the target equals the caller's own persona" - breaks today's only working path: a reader's `sess.persona` *is* the persona it reads (`hooks/index.ts:1033-1036` joins as reader under `sess.persona`; `:3722` and `:3765` default the target to `sess.persona`), so that reading would deny the Reviewer's own `reader:dev` session from ever messaging `dev`. The real guard is narrower: denied only when the caller **owns** the target persona (`sess.isOwner` true and target equals `sess.persona`) - an owner talking to itself, which is pointless. A reader addressing the persona it holds a reader claim on is unaffected, exactly as today. An owner addressing a *different* persona than its own is not self-messaging and reaches the next check below.

**Reaching a persona the caller doesn't own or hold a reader claim on (the actual coordinator mechanism).** Neither `agentic_say`'s owner-only denial (`hooks/index.ts:3731-3733`) nor `agentic_inbox`'s matching one (`:3768-3771`) is the whole story once a `persona` argument can name a target the caller neither owns nor reads: allowed when either the caller holds `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` (coordinator addressing any worker), **or** the target equals `<coordinatorPersona>` and the caller owns some persona of its own (any worker escalating to the coordinator, no special claim beyond being a legitimate live persona owner - the coordinator's own name is the address). `cfg.coordinatorPersona` is read beside `controllerTickMs` (`hooks/index.ts:648`).

**The delivery-side gates (Reviewer Round 109 R8/F1, F4 - the critical gap the first revision only asserted, never actually composed).** Being allowed to *send* is not being allowed to *arrive*: `hasLiveReaderClaim` (`hooks/operator.ts:407-417`) matches only a `reader:<persona>` resource, full stop - it has no notion of a coordinator claim at all, so a worker's record addressed to the coordinator (`from` = the worker's own session, holding `persona:<worker>`, never `reader:coordinator`) is refused at the send gate, or, if it somehow got past that, marked `"skipped"` at the tick's delivery filter. Five call sites read this gate today and all five need a second, coordinator-aware check added beside the existing `hasLiveReaderClaim` call, not instead of it: `agentic_say`'s send gate (`:3736-3740`), `agentic_inbox`'s matching gate (`:3773-3777`), the tick's delivery filter (`:1336`, marks `"skipped"` at `:1345`), the urgent break-in (`:3846`), and the ask-answer delivery path (`:1266`, drops a coordinator's answer to a worker's open ask with `operator_skipped_no_claim` today - the fifth gate the first revision missed entirely). A new exported helper beside `hasLiveReaderClaim`, `hasLiveOwnerClaim(store, sessionId, persona?, opts?: { excludePersona?: string })` (checks `readAllClaims` for a live `persona:<persona>` claim held by `sessionId`; when `persona` is omitted, any live `persona:*` claim at all held by that session, except one matching `opts.excludePersona` when given), composes at each of the five sites as: `hasLiveReaderClaim(store, persona, rec.from) || hasLiveOwnerClaim(store, rec.from, coordinatorPersona) || (persona === coordinatorPersona && hasLiveOwnerClaim(store, rec.from, { excludePersona: "default" }))` - the first disjunct is today's existing reader path (untouched), the second is the coordinator addressing any worker, the third is any worker pushing to the coordinator, deliberately excluding a bare `persona:default` claim from satisfying it. **The `!== "default"` exclusion in the third disjunct (Reviewer Round 113 R44) is load-bearing until Section 6 lands:** every plugin-loaded interactive session claims `persona:default` today (Section 5's own ordering note says as much), so without this exclusion any plain chat session on this machine could push text into the coordinator's model the moment it exists, on no stronger a credential than having the plugin loaded at all. Excluding `persona:default` keeps out a session that has issued no claim beyond the one it gets at start, and nothing more: every ground the gate accepts is a claim a session issues itself, since `agentic_identity` lets any plugin-loaded session own any unheld named persona, `claimReaderRole` is ungated, and the coordinator persona is claimable while no live session holds it. The boundary this section ships is therefore any plugin-loaded process on this machine, stated as such in the README; Section 6's arming tiers are what narrow it (corrected at execution, 2026-09-14: the earlier wording claimed the exclusion admitted only a launched worker).

Files in scope: `hooks/index.ts` (tool registrations and handlers for both tools, all five delivery-side gates named above, and `cfg.coordinatorPersona`'s read site beside `controllerTickMs`), `hooks/operator.ts` (the write/read helpers both tools call, and the new reach helper beside `hasLiveReaderClaim`: shipped as `mayReachPersonaIn` over one claims read plus `mayReachPersona`, which composes the three legs above; the `hasLiveOwnerClaim` name this text proposed was folded into that helper's private predicate), `README.md` (the Operator channel section, which today states both tools are reader-only and refused to the owner, and states the trust boundary as "a record whose writer holds a reader claim on the same persona" - `README.md:277-287, 318` - both need updating to state the coordinator exception).
Tests: both directions of the corrected self-message guard (an owner cannot message its own persona; a reader still can address the persona it reads, unaffected - this is the control that catches the F2 regression before it ships); both directions of the coordinator-claim requirement for reaching an unrelated third persona (refused without the claim, succeeds with it); a **delivery** test, not merely a send test, for the worker-to-coordinator direction: a worker's record addressed to the coordinator is actually submitted to the coordinator's model on the next tick (not marked `"skipped"`), and the same for the ask-answer path - a send-only test is exactly what let this gap ship once already; and, per R44, a control that a session holding only `persona:default` is refused on the worker-to-coordinator path even though it holds a live owner claim, since that claim alone is not proof of being a real worker.

### 4. Provenance labels: `[COORDINATOR]`, `[READER:<persona>]`, `[OPERATOR]` reserved; the urgent break-in stops crossing into subagents
Model: opus
At both delivery sites (`hooks/index.ts:1372`, `:3860`) and the ask-answer delivery (`:1323`), the label is computed at delivery time (not stored on the record at send) from the writer's live commons claims: `[COORDINATOR]` when the writer holds `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` (the config key Section 3 defines); `[READER:<persona>]` for any other reader, where `<persona>` is the persona the writer holds a live *reader claim* on (not necessarily the record's target - relevant once Section 3's coordinator exception lets a caller address a persona it neither owns nor reads); when the writer holds several reader claims at once (the Approach notes the Reviewer holds three today), the label names the record's target persona if it is among them, else the first one alphabetically (Reviewer Round 111 R26); `[WORKER:<persona>]` for a writer whose only live claim is an owned, non-coordinator persona - Section 3's third disjunct (a worker pushing to the coordinator) produces exactly this shape, holding `persona:<worker>` and no reader claim, and without this label it would fall through to the untouched `[OPERATOR]` literal, laundering a worker's own escalation as operator authority - the exact provenance gap this section exists to close (Reviewer Round 113 R33); `[OPERATOR]` reserved for the operator's own Discord-originated channel path, which does not go through this delivery code today and is untouched by this section. Uses `readAllClaims` (`hooks/commons.ts:154`), already used elsewhere for commons arbitration.

Round 106's live incident is folded in here rather than left as a separate gap: the item 8.3 urgent break-in (`hooks/index.ts:3830-3868`) fires on the `tool.call` hook, which runs for a dispatched subagent's own tool calls exactly as it does for the top-level session's - so a Reviewer's urgent steer was delivered as `[OPERATOR, urgent]` context inside a running `implementer-opus` subagent's `Edit` call, which correctly could not verify it and refused it. Fix: the urgent break-in only fires when the `tool.call` event can be confirmed to belong to the top-level session, not a subagent's own call; if the event carries no field distinguishing the two (checked against the real `ToolCallEvent` shape at implementation time, not assumed here), the break-in is disabled entirely for that call and the record falls back to ordinary tick-based delivery at the owner's next quiet moment - a safe default over a mislabelled one.

Files in scope: `hooks/index.ts` (the delivery sites and the urgent break-in's session-scope check); `hooks/operator.ts` (the label computation, exported beside `mayReachPersonaIn` so the label and the reach gate read one claims array through one rule; widened at execution, 2026-09-14); `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs` (the existing assertion at `:672-673` that the general drain produces exactly one `[OPERATOR]`-prefixed prompt is updated to expect `[READER:<persona>]` for a plain reader, and the assertions at `:1528` and `:4563` that also key on the literal `[OPERATOR]` string; three more negative-control assertions at `:858-859`, `:925-926`, and `:4572` each assert *zero* `[OPERATOR]`-prefixed prompts - after the relabel every one of these passes vacuously regardless of what the drain actually did, since a `[READER:...]`-labelled prompt still isn't `[OPERATOR]`, so each is rewritten to assert zero prompts carrying the reader's text under *any* label, not just that one); `.kit/live-restartrequest-test.sh` (its standing instruction at `:77-81` is keyed on the literal `[OPERATOR]` prefix and is updated to match whichever label that suite's own reader now carries, or the suite's reader is given the coordinator claim, whichever keeps its restart proof honest); `.kit/live-operator-test.sh` (its assertion at `:576-600` fails a run whose reader text lacks the literal `[OPERATOR] ` prefix and needs the same update); the `operator_delivered` decision's own `detail` string, `"submitted as [OPERATOR]"` at `hooks/index.ts:1370` (and the stale comments at `:1250, 1297` referencing the same literal), which after the relabel records a label the prompt no longer carries - fixed to name whichever label was actually submitted (Reviewer Round 113 R40; `.kit/assert-decisions.js:474` doesn't key on this string, so the drift is a stale record, not a red test, which is exactly why it needs a deliberate fix rather than relying on a suite to catch it); `README.md:312, 314, 318` (documents the `[OPERATOR]` marker and the reader-claim trust boundary; updated to describe all three labels and the coordinator exception).
Tests: one case per label (coordinator claim present -> `[COORDINATOR]`; a plain reader claim, no coordinator -> `[READER:<persona>]`); a control that the operator's own Discord-origin path still carries no prefix at all (that path only sets a flag, `hooks/index.ts:3882`; it was never prefixed, and this section leaves it that way); and, for the urgent-break-in fix, a case that a subagent's own tool call never receives break-in context (falls back to tick delivery) with a control that the top-level session's tool call still does.

### 5. Contention guards
Model: sonnet
No new knob: `controllerTickMs` is already a per-launch setting (`bin/supervise.sh:119` emits it from the `TICK_MS`/`controllerTickMs` env var into the child's settings, which `hooks/index.ts:648` reads and which beats the code's own 30000 default). The coordinator's launch recipe (Section 6) simply sets it higher at launch - 60000, six times `bin/supervise.sh`'s own 10000 default - rather than inventing a second, competing knob. `.kit/live-all.sh`'s running-lock is extended: today `wait_persona_free` (`bin/agentic-common.sh:137-169`) *waits*, polling for up to a timeout, for any live claim on the literal resource `persona:default` (`:164`) to clear - it does not refuse to start, and it only ever checks that one hardcoded resource. The fix is a genuine addition, not a widening of the existing wait: a new refuse-at-start check, beside the existing `.kit/RUNNING` lock-file check, that fails the run immediately (no polling wait) if any `persona:` claim at all is currently live in the shared commons store - the whole gate cannot run at all while a real fleet is up, full stop, rather than waiting out someone else's claim and possibly starting inside its own 90-second stale window.

**Both stores, not one (Reviewer Round 113 R32).** `.kit/live-all.sh` pins its own pre-gate to the inline (`--plugin-dir`) store and errors if `find_global_store` ever resolves anything else (`.kit/live-all.sh:54, 61-70`) - correct for a suite that only ever launches `--plugin-dir` children itself, but Section 0 item 5 moves every worker and the coordinator to the *installed*-mode store. A claim planted only in the installed store would satisfy this refuse-at-start check by never being seen: the check reads both `find_global_store 1` (inline) and `find_global_store 0` (installed) and refuses on a live `persona:` claim in either, not just the one the suite's own children happen to use.
Files in scope: `bin/supervise.sh` (the coordinator's own `TICK_MS` at launch - a Section 6 concern, noted here for completeness), `bin/agentic-common.sh` (a new refuse-at-start check, not a change to `wait_persona_free` itself), `.kit/live-all.sh` (wiring the new check in beside `.kit/RUNNING`), `README.md:449` (the runner lock this section changes; Reviewer Round 111 R30, corrected per Round 113 R41 - `:221` describes the pre-gate, which this section does not change, and is dropped from scope). Widened at execution, 2026-09-14: `.kit/persona-live-refuse-test.sh` (the stub-driven suite the Tests line earns) and `.kit/.gitignore` (the one exception line that lets that suite be tracked).

**Ordering consequence (Reviewer Round 109 F11):** until Section 6's arming default lands, *any* interactive session on this machine that loads the plugin claims `persona:default` on session start, which means this section's refuse-at-start check fires on that ordinary claim too - a plain chat session left open is enough to block the whole gate from starting, same as a real worker would. This is a known, temporary interaction, not a bug to fix in this section: Section 6's arming default removes the ordinary-session `persona:default` claim entirely, and until it lands, "the machine is otherwise quiet" (Section 0's own wording) means every plugin-loaded session, chat or worker, is closed.
Tests: a case that the new refuse-at-start check fails immediately with one live persona claim present, and a control that it proceeds clean with zero.

### 6. Coordinator launch shape, config key, and persona/thread naming
Model: sonnet
The coordinator's persona name is read as `cfg.coordinatorPersona` beside `controllerTickMs` (`hooks/index.ts:648`'s config-reading site), default `coordinator`, settable the same way `cfg.persona` already is. The coordinator is launched exactly like a worker - `bin/supervise.sh <repo> <persona> <permission-mode> --rundir <path>` per the memory record `a-second-worker-launches-in-its-own-repo-with-a-unique-persona-name` - with `MODEL` set to the Fable model id, `<persona>` set to the same name as `coordinatorPersona` (or `coordinatorPersona` overridden to match, whichever the operator finds clearer), `TICK_MS`/`controllerTickMs` set to Section 5's higher value, and its own `--channel-name` (its own Discord thread, distinct from every worker's). `emit_settings_json` also exports `COORDINATOR_PERSONA` as an env var carrying the same value it writes into settings as `coordinatorPersona` (Reviewer Round 113 R37 - `bin/supervise.sh` itself never reads plugin config, so Section 8's own priming-injection check compares `$PERSONA` against this env var rather than against a settings value the shell script has no way to read). No new script. The launch recipe omits `--dev`: Section 0 item 5 retires the `--plugin-dir` runtime for the installed marketplace plugin as a v2 prerequisite, and every worker and the coordinator load the same installed copy, per that section. This section also checks `bin/supervise.sh` for any assumption that a worker never messages other personas, and lifts it if found (checked against the actual script, not assumed).

Also promoted here from the backlog, per Reviewer Round 105 R5 rather than left as a separate future brainstorm: every session that loads this plugin fires its hooks and its goal-nudge reminders on every turn, including a plain interactive chat session with no persona work to do - already felt on the Reviewer's own seat today. v2 adds more sessions loading the plugin (the coordinator, more workers), which makes the problem more common, not less.

**Three tiers, not one armed/unarmed bit (Reviewer Round 109 R11/F7 correction).** A single on/off key forces a choice neither side wants: a fully armed reader seat (this Reviewer's own shape) gets nagged with goal-tree reminders it has no tree to act on, and a fully disarmed one loses `agentic_identity`/`agentic_say`/`agentic_inbox` - the tools a reader actually needs to steer. Three values for the arming key: `off` (no persona tools registered at all - a plain chat/analysis session, the common case this requirement exists for); `reader` (commons/identity/`agentic_say`/`agentic_inbox` registered; no goal-tree tools, no nudges, no backfill, no channel backstop - the Reviewer's own shape); `owner` (everything - the worker and coordinator shape). This section's launch recipe sets `owner` for every worker and coordinator it starts; a reader session (like the Reviewer's) sets `reader` explicitly rather than inheriting whatever `off`'s default leaves it holding.

**`off` must not merely skip tool registration (Reviewer Round 111 R20 correction).** The session-start claim (`claimResource` on `persona:${sess.persona}`, `hooks/index.ts:1069-1072`) and its heartbeat-tick re-claim (`:1128`) fire unconditionally today, outside any tool call - an `off` session that only skips tool registration still claims `persona:default` in the shared commons store, which means Section 5's refuse-at-start check still blocks the whole gate on an ordinary chat session's presence, the exact problem this arming requirement exists to remove. `off` must also skip both claim sites; its own acceptance test checks the commons store directly, not merely that no persona tool was registered.

The exact mechanism (a `register()`-time branch on the arming key, gating which tools get registered, which hook bodies run past an early return, and whether the two claim sites above fire at all) is this section's own design work, not fixed here.
Files in scope: `bin/agentic-common.sh`'s `emit_settings_json` (`:35-97`, the real config-emission site every live suite calls - `.kit/live-operator-test.sh:107`, `.kit/live-commons-test.sh:128, 205`, `.kit/live-controller-test.sh:49`, and the rest - not `bin/supervise.sh` as the prior revision named; each suite's call sets `owner` so the whole gate's own behavior is unchanged), `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs` (the harness's own `OPTS` at `:35` also needs the arming key set to `owner`, or the harness itself goes red the moment this section lands, since its fake `$` never emits settings through `emit_settings_json` at all), `hooks/index.ts` (`cfg.coordinatorPersona`'s read site, and the new arming check at the top of `register()`, including the two claim sites named above), `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (the new arming key, following the existing convention that `askReraiseWindowMs` and its siblings already use - `askReraiseWindowMs` itself does have a `plugin.json` entry at `:60`, so this section's new key is documented there the same way), `README.md` (the quickstart gains the coordinator launch shape and the arming requirement as documented patterns, per item 6 of v1 - `README.md:285`'s own drift is Section 2's to fix, not this section's).
Tests: for the arming tiers, a case per tier - `off` never registers any persona tool **and** the commons store holds no claim from that session after `session.start` and a heartbeat tick (the R20 correction, checked directly against the store, not inferred from tool registration); `reader` registers commons/identity/`agentic_say`/`agentic_inbox` but never a goal-tree tool or a nudge/reminder injection; `owner` registers everything, matching today's unconditional behavior exactly (the control that this section changes nothing for an existing worker). For the launch shape, no harness test; acceptance is proved live: the launched process holds `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` in the commons store, its own Discord thread opens, and one `agentic_inbox` call with `persona: <a live worker's name>` returns the coordinator's own records addressed to that worker (not that worker's records generally - `agentic_inbox` filters to the caller's own sent records, `hooks/index.ts:3780`, and this section's acceptance is written against that real behavior), with `reply` and `deferred` fields populated where applicable.

### 7. FORK B's mechanism: the worker-side standing instruction and the bound checks
Model: fable
Nothing built by Sections 1-6 actually implements the Decisions section's FORK B ruling (Reviewer Round 109 F3): a `[COORDINATOR]`-labelled record still arrives as a plain plugin-submitted prompt the model has never been told carries any special standing, and live evidence already shows what happens without one - a subagent shown `[OPERATOR, urgent]` text with no instruction backing it (Round 106) correctly treated it as unverified. Two halves, both needed before Option 2 does anything:
1. **The worker-side standing instruction.** A fixed sentence, injected on the priming turn beside `CHANNEL_REPLY_INSTRUCTION` (`bin/supervise.sh:393`) - the correct site for a standing instruction; `REPLY_INSTRUCTION` (`hooks/index.ts:346`) is a different mechanism with different reach, prepended to every `$.prompt.submit` text rather than stated once at start (Reviewer Round 111 R27 correction) - telling the worker's own model: a prompt prefixed `[COORDINATOR]` carries the operator's own delegated authority for anything that ties to a goal node in the worker's approved plan and stays within that node's scope (the commit-model and exclusion-list clauses this sentence once carried are struck by the 2026-09-14 revision in item 2); anything outside that bound is surfaced to the operator exactly as an unlabelled steer would be. This is the actual mechanism that turns Option 2 from a policy on paper into worker behavior. **The priming turn does not always exist (Reviewer Round 113 R36 correction):** `bin/supervise.sh:397-421` writes one only when a `PROMPT_FILE` exists or `NO_CHANNEL` is not `1` - a worker launched `--no-channel` with no prompt file, exactly the shape the `.kit/live-*` suites run, gets neither branch and no priming turn at all. This section's own instruction rides on its own priming write, one that fires regardless of `NO_CHANNEL`, mirroring how `.kit/channel-reply-instruction-test.sh` already tests the reply instruction under both values.
2. **No bound checks (revised 2026-09-14, FORK B's revision paragraph).** The four ask-first items are struck by the operator's ruling, so the plugin refuses nothing inside a coordinator-origin turn and tracks no coordinator-origin flag. The acts that must stay impossible are external controls: the repository's branch protection and the pull-request review, which the session cannot approve for itself. The mechanical bound built across this section's first five review rounds (the guarded-path, commit-model, push and merge arms, the origin flags and their lifecycle) is removed whole, and `hooks/index.ts`, `hooks/operator.ts` and `hooks/self-review.ts` return to their pre-section state. The standing instruction's sentence names no exclusion list and no refusal; it states the delegated authority, the scope tie to the plan's goal node, the urgent and READER/WORKER exclusions, and the resolve call.
3. **The urgent path is excluded from Option 2 entirely (Reviewer Round 113 R35 - author's call, not a sentence-level fix).** The urgent break-in delivers text as `context` on a tool result (`hooks/index.ts:3860-3865`), which the operating doctrine's own rule on embedded text says to surface, never act on - exactly the shape Round 106 already proved a subagent correctly refuses, and moving the break-in off subagents (Section 4) does not change what the *top-level* model sees when the same shape reaches it directly. Rather than route an urgent coordinator steer through `$.prompt.submit` instead (a real option, more invasive - it would open a new turn mid-turn), this spec keeps `urgent` scoped to what it already is: a stop-or-redirect signal the worker surfaces to itself and decides on its own judgment, never a channel for Option 2's delegated authority. A stop needs no delegated authority to act on - surfacing it is enough. Only non-urgent, tick-delivered `[COORDINATOR]` records - which arrive as an ordinary `$.prompt.submit` turn, not tool-result context - carry Option 2's authority.
The standing instruction also carries Section 12's resolve sentence, per the Standing Brief Amendments entry naming it.
Files in scope: `bin/supervise.sh` (the standing instruction's own priming-turn write, independent of `NO_CHANNEL`), `hooks/index.ts`, `hooks/operator.ts`, `hooks/self-review.ts` (restored to the pre-section state), `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs` (the bound's harness cases removed), `.kit/channel-reply-instruction-test.sh` (the steer sentence's checks).
Tests (split per Reviewer Round 111 R28 - a harness with a fake `$` and no model cannot observe model behavior, and per Round 113 R36 this section's own priming-turn test is a bash-level case, not a `controller-tick-test.mjs` one): a `.kit/*-test.sh` case that the standing instruction is injected on its own priming write for both `NO_CHANNEL` values, mirroring `.kit/channel-reply-instruction-test.sh`'s own shape. The bound-check harness cases the earlier text named are struck with the bound. Whether a non-urgent `[COORDINATOR]`-labelled record tied to an active goal node actually gets acted on directly, versus the same content unlabelled producing an operator round trip instead, is model behavior and is a live case under `.kit/live-*`, not a harness case.

### 8. The coordinator's own standing instructions (Reviewer Round 111 R19)
Model: fable
Section 7 builds only the worker-side half of FORK B. Nothing built anywhere in this spec tells the coordinator's own model what it is: followed exactly as written, Section 6 launches a Fable process holding `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` that has never been instructed it is a coordinator, carries a round cap, or should batch its steers. This section is that missing coordinator-side instruction, injected on the coordinator's own priming turn (Section 7's own fix - the standing priming write independent of `NO_CHANNEL` - applies here identically), stating: the two-round cap per steer and the escalation criterion from Decisions item 3 (raise a steer to the operator directly, on the coordinator's own thread, rather than push a third round or force a worker past its plan's scope); the batching rule from the Approach's own operating rule (steers to one worker batch into one record per cycle; `urgent` is reserved for a real stop-or-redirect; status is read from files, never asked of a worker in-band) - stated nowhere else as an instruction to follow, only as a design rationale in prose until this section; and that it labels its own outbound records so a worker can apply Section 7's bound (which, mechanically, is just being the session that holds `persona:<coordinatorPersona>` - nothing extra for the coordinator to do here beyond existing as that session).

**`bin/supervise.sh` cannot compare against `cfg.coordinatorPersona` because it never reads plugin config at all (Reviewer Round 113 R37 correction).** The launch script holds `$PERSONA` (its own CLI argument) and has no visibility into the plugin's own settings JSON it emits - Section 6 itself allows the CLI persona name and `coordinatorPersona` to differ, so `$PERSONA` alone is not a safe proxy either. Fix: Section 6's `emit_settings_json` call also exports a `COORDINATOR_PERSONA` env var alongside the settings it already writes, and `bin/supervise.sh` compares its own `$PERSONA` against that env var (not against plugin config it cannot read) to decide whether this section's priming injection applies to this particular launch.
The coordinator instruction also carries Section 12's resolve sentence, per the Standing Brief Amendments entry naming it, so rounds per steer are counted against resolutions.
Files in scope: `bin/supervise.sh` (the coordinator-priming-turn injection site, mirroring Section 7's own, gated on the `COORDINATOR_PERSONA` comparison above); `bin/agentic-common.sh` (the provided-settings read of `coordinatorPersona`, widened at execution); `.kit/channel-reply-instruction-test.sh` and `.kit/settings-plugin-key-test.sh` (the bash-level cases, widened at execution); `docs/backlog.md` (one paragraph on the routed finding, widened at execution).
Tests: a `.kit/*-test.sh` case (per Section 7's R36 correction, this is a bash-level test, not a harness case) that a launch with `$PERSONA` matching `COORDINATOR_PERSONA` injects this instruction on its own priming turn, with a control that an ordinary worker's launch (`$PERSONA` not matching) does not. The round-cap and batching *behavior* is model behavior, live-suite territory per R28's same reasoning, not a test-file assertion.

### 9. An unmatched turn completion still corrupts the idle reading and the deferred-status stamp
Model: opus

Surfaced by Section 0's own work on the controller's in-flight test, and appended as approval drift rather than folded, because it needs acceptance criteria Section 0 does not carry.

The controller decides a worker is idle from `sess.state.monitor.lastTurnComplete`, and a reader session reports how long an inbox record has waited from `sess.turnStartedAt`. Both are written unconditionally at the top of the `turn.complete` handler in `hooks/index.ts`, before anything checks whether this completion matches a turn that was actually open. Confirmed in the `turn.complete` handler, which writes both before it checks anything about the arriving completion.

The turn events are not reliably paired. One observed window carries two `turn.complete` events with no `turn.start` between them. So an unmatched completion restarts the idle clock while a turn is still running, clears the start stamp a reader needs, and leaves the long-turn record measuring against a different turn's start.

Section 11 makes the in-flight reading survive unpaired events by keying it on turn ids. These two fields are the remainder: the same handler, the same premise, and still corrupted by the same shape.

Two further defects in the same handler, routed here from Section 0's review rounds rather than fixed there.

`sess.turnStartedAt` is a single value while the open-turn map is keyed by turn id. Whichever completion arrives first nulls it, so with two overlapping turns the second completion reads it as null and its long-turn record is skipped. The stamp becomes derived from the map rather than cleared, through one helper both turn handlers call, so a second turn opening cannot move it either. The long-turn record stops depending on the stamp at all: `turn.complete` carries `durationMs`, the harness's own measurement of that turn, which counts a long turn whose start this session never saw. Nothing else in the plugin reads that field, so the map entry stays as a fallback rather than letting an absent field switch the record off silently.

The third defect this section was written to fix is already closed. `currentPrompt` and `nudgedTurn` were written after `await $.prompt.submit`, so a promise settling only once the nudged turn had run would set `nudgedTurn` after the `turn.complete` that reads it and leak the flag into the following turn. Section 11 moved all three writes, the nudge floor with them, to the synchronous side of the submit (the nudge branch of the controller tick, which writes the floor, the prompt text, the nudged-turn flag and the escalation count before the submit), for that same reason stated in its own comment. Nothing here is left to do, and this section does not touch that code.

Files in scope: `hooks/index.ts` (the `turn.complete` handler), `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs`.

Tests: an unmatched completion leaves `sess.turnStartedAt` naming a turn that is still open, with a withheld control that a matched completion for the only open turn sets it to null; a second turn opening leaves the stamp at the earlier turn's start; the long-turn record measures against its own turn rather than another turn's clock across an interleaved unmatched completion, counts an unmatched long turn when the harness supplies `durationMs`, and writes nothing when neither that field nor a map entry is there. `lastTurnComplete` is pinned unchanged by its own case, as a regression guard on the Decisions ruling below rather than coverage of a change.

Acceptance:

1. A completion for a turn id this session never saw start leaves `sess.turnStartedAt` naming a turn that is still open.
2. A completion for the only open turn sets `sess.turnStartedAt` to null.
3. With two turns open, a completion for the later one leaves the stamp at the earlier turn's start, and a second turn opening does not move it either.
4. The long-turn record measures the completing turn rather than another turn's clock.
5. The long-turn record is written for a completion whose turn this session never saw start when the harness supplies a duration, and written for no completion where neither that duration nor a map entry is available.
6. `sess.state.monitor.lastTurnComplete` is updated by every completion, matched or not.

Bullets 1 through 4 and 6 restate what this section's own text above already asks for. Bullet 5 is wider than that text, which named only the map entry: the harness duration was adopted from a review finding during execution, because a record depending on the map alone is blind on a session where most starts are never delivered. That widening is recorded as drift in this section's Chapter rather than left to read as original scope.

The open question this section carried, whether `lastTurnComplete` should update on an unmatched completion, is answered under `## Decisions` below: it should, it is left exactly as it is, and only `sess.turnStartedAt` and the long-turn record change.

What the harness guarantees, confirmed at `.claude/types/claude-code.d.ts:6532-6552`: `turn.complete` carries its answer, duration, interrupt flag, turn id and cost "whatever its reason", and the `aborted` flag is true when the turn ended by interruption. So an interrupted turn still completes, and a completion is not lost to an abort. That narrows the missing-completion case to a failure below the harness, such as the host process dying, and it is the citation behind Section 0's decision to bound the open-turn map rather than trust pairing.

### 10. `goal_add` activates the node it creates when nothing else is active
Model: sonnet

This is the source fix for the `goal_add` activation defect `docs/backlog.md` records. It is small, and it is why workers were not closing goal nodes.

`goal_add` activates its new node only when `kind === "task"` and the parent is already `active` (`hooks/index.ts:3485-3494`). A `plan` node takes no such branch and returns "planning or activation will occur at the next tick" (`:3505`). The tick is the only other activator, and its order is owner check, then in-flight check, then planning gate (`:1250`), with the early return at `:1258`, so the planning gate cannot run while a turn is open.

A plan node added during a turn therefore cannot be activated until that turn ends. When the work it names finishes in that same turn, `goal_done` finds no active leaf and denies at `:3616`, and the node stays `pending` forever. `docs/backlog.md` records four reproductions in one session. Shortening the tick interval does not help, because no tick reaches the gate.

Fix: when the tree has no active leaf, `goal_add` activates the node it just created rather than deferring, mirroring the `task` branch above it. It activates that node directly rather than calling `activateNext`, whose walk returns the oldest eligible leaf and so would hand activation to some other node. What it reuses instead is `activateNext`’s eligibility rule, which decides not ordering but which nodes may be active at all: a node must be pending, must be a leaf, and every ancestor below the root must be pending too. That rule is exported from `hooks/agent-state.ts` as `isActivationEligible`, and `activateNext` reads it too, so there is one definition rather than two.

The branch does not fire while the controller holds the tree: an open operator question, or a node paused by the nudge cap. A node paused any other way does not hold it, because an operator can pause one node while another keeps working.

Decide at implementation time and record it: whether a `plan` node with no children is activated directly, or whether activation waits for its first child task. The tree's own shape rules are in `activateNext`'s DFS, which only ever activates leaves, so the answer probably falls out of reusing it.

Files in scope: `hooks/index.ts` (the `goal_add` handler), `hooks/agent-state.ts` (folded in during execution, for the eligibility rule above), `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs`.

Tests: a plan node added with no active leaf is active when `goal_add` returns, and `goal_done` closes it in the same turn with no tick in between. Controls: adding under an active parent still demotes and activates as it does today, and the tick's own planning gate still activates when the node was added with no turn open.

### 11. The goal nudge is bounded by an open-turn reading that survives unpaired events
Model: opus

Appended as approval drift rather than folded, because Section 0 carries this work and states no acceptance for it. Section 0's own six items never mention the controller's in-flight reading at all, so the work had no specification to be judged against. This section is that specification.

Two defects, both observed rather than inferred.

A long turn collected one queued `[GOAL]` nudge per controller tick, 48 of them in a single measured window. The cause is that `sess.lastNudgeAt` was written after `await $.prompt.submit`, and that promise does not settle while the turn it waits on is still running. Every tick in that window therefore read the same stale floor, passed it, and queued another identical copy.

The turn events are not reliably paired, on Section 9's own evidence: one window carries two `turn.complete` events with no `turn.start` between them. A single boolean carries only the last event, so any one completion reads as "no turn is open" however many turns are still running.

Fix: the in-flight reading is keyed by turn id rather than held as a boolean, and the nudge floor is spent before the submit rather than after it.

No age-out bounds the open-turn map. Three fix rounds built one and each reopened the defect beside it, and a scope ruling on 2026-09-13 refused the mechanism: nothing in the Goal asks for it, and the map is in-process state that a lost completion could only outlive on host death, which takes the map with it. See the Decisions entry.

Files in scope: `hooks/index.ts`, `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs`, `.kit/tick-harness.mjs`.

Acceptance:

1. No nudge is submitted while a turn is open, and that reading is correct when a completion arrives for a turn whose start was never seen.
2. The nudge floor is spent before the submit, so a submit that does not settle during the turn cannot leave the next tick reading an unspent floor.
3. The escalation counter and the nudged-turn flag are spent before the submit alongside the floor, so a promise that settles after the turn cannot let a compliant worker accumulate the counter past its cap.

Tests: a case per criterion, each watched red before green. Criterion 1 takes a withheld control, a completion for a turn whose start was never seen leaving the reading unchanged.

### 12. Inbox upkeep: a handled state, cleanup that never loses unread work, and a reply link no other turn can take
Model: opus

Appended by the operator's decision of 2026-09-13, recorded under `## Decisions`. It runs after Sections 1 and 2 and before Section 3, because Section 3 is where the coordinator starts addressing workers by name and depends on this lifecycle. The section number is its decomposition, not its build order.

The inbox records read, replied and linked-reply, and it bounds its own size. Three gaps remain, each confirmed in the code on `item0-5-installed-runtime` at `97a7765`.

**No handled state.** `InboxStatus` is `pending | delivered | answered | skipped` (`hooks/operator.ts:19`). `answered` is written when the turn stamped with the record ends with a non-empty answer (`hooks/index.ts:3181-3194`). That means a turn replied, not that the work finished. A steer taking three turns reads `answered` after the first. The coordinator's two-round cap per steer (Decisions, FORK B item 3) needs to count finished steers, and it cannot.

**Cleanup can lose unread work, and loses it unlogged.** `sweepExpiredRecords` deletes every `inbox:` record older than the TTL, 24 hours by default, whatever its status, `pending` included (`hooks/operator.ts:277-285`). It runs before `enforceChannelWindow` on the same cadence (`hooks/index.ts:1435` then `:1457`). So a record that ages out is deleted outright and never reaches `.agentic-channel.jsonl`, and only a count survives in the decision log.

**The reply link can be taken by another turn (inferred, not reproduced).** The first `turn.start` after a delivery stamps its turn id onto any `delivered` record with no turn id (`hooks/index.ts:2740-2750`). Nothing checks that this turn is the one the plugin opened for that record. A turn opened from the operator's Discord channel, or a `[GOAL]` nudge, starting first would take the stamp, and its answer would be filed as the reply. The channel-origin flag this needs already exists: `currentTurnIsChannelOrigin`, set at `turn.start` (`:2721`). Confirming the race live is part of this section's red step.

Fix:
1. **A resolution the worker sets.** An owner-side tool, `agentic_resolve`, takes a record id, an outcome of `done` or `declined`, and a short note. It writes `resolvedAt`, `outcome` and `note` onto that record and sets status `resolved`. It is refused unless `sess.isOwner` is true and the record's persona equals `sess.persona`, which keeps a reader session holding the persona from resolving, and it is refused for a record still `pending`. It registers under Section 6's `owner` tier only. `agentic_inbox` returns `outcome`, `note` and `resolvedAt` beside `reply` for each of the caller's records. The Section 7 standing instruction and the Section 8 coordinator instruction each gain one sentence: a worker resolves a coordinator record when the work is finished or declined, and the coordinator counts rounds against resolutions rather than replies.
2. **Cleanup that keeps unread work and logs before it deletes.** The TTL sweep never touches a `pending` record.
