A pending record already leaves the queue by the existing route when its writer has no live claim (`skipped`, `hooks/index.ts:1380-1381`), so a pending record that survives is live work. Every record the TTL removes is appended to `.agentic-channel.jsonl` before it is deleted, on the same append-before-delete contract `enforceChannelWindow` holds (`hooks/operator.ts:364-374`), and a failed append leaves the record in the store and logs the refusal. The window roll keeps `delivered` and `answered` records that are not yet resolved in the store rather than rolling them, so an open steer's state stays readable. The TTL is the bound on those.
3. **A reply link only the plugin's own turn can take.** Both tick delivery sites, the general drain (`hooks/index.ts:1393-1408`) and the ask-answer delivery (`:1322-1359`), record which record they submit before they call `$.prompt.submit`, on the synchronous side, the same reason Section 11 moved its writes there. `turn.start` stamps only that record, and only on a turn flagged neither channel-origin nor nudged; a turn opened by a goal nudge is read from the nudged-turn flag. The mechanism is checked against the real turn event type at implementation time. Where nothing distinguishes the plugin's turn from another, the stamp is withheld rather than guessed. The record then stays `delivered` with no reply, which the sender reads as unanswered rather than as a wrong answer. The abort re-stamp at `turn.complete` (`:3203-3208`), which clears the turn id so the next turn re-stamps, is retired with it: an aborted delivery stays `delivered` with no reply until the TTL.

If the live race in the defect paragraph does not reproduce, the harness case is the red step and the Chapter records the race as inferred rather than confirmed.

Files in scope: `hooks/operator.ts`, `hooks/index.ts`, `hooks/self-review.ts` (a noise-list ruling for each new decision action, as Chapter 2 folded it for Section 11), `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs`, `.kit/tick-harness.mjs`, `.kit/commons-unit-test.mjs`, `README.md` (the Operator channel section's Tools, Record shapes, Bounded store and Delivery text, `README.md:271-314`).

Acceptance:
1. A worker's `agentic_resolve` on a delivered or answered record addressed to its persona sets status `resolved` with the outcome and note, and the sender's `agentic_inbox` returns them.
2. `agentic_resolve` is refused for a `pending` record and for a record addressed to another persona.
3. A `pending` record older than the TTL is still in the store after the sweep.
4. Every record the TTL sweep removes appears in `.agentic-channel.jsonl` first, and a failed append leaves it in the store.
5. The window roll does not remove a `delivered` or `answered` record that is not resolved.
6. A turn flagged channel-origin, a turn opened by a goal nudge, or any other turn the plugin did not open for the record, starting after a delivery does not take the record's stamp, and its answer is not written as that record's reply.
7. The plugin's own delivery turn still takes the stamp and writes the reply, as today, at both the general drain and the ask-answer delivery.

Tests: a case per acceptance bullet, each watched red before green. Bullet 3 and bullet 6 each take a withheld control: an old non-pending record is swept, and the plugin's own turn is stamped.

### 13. Test audit: every suite earns its place against the test bar
Model: opus

Appended by the operator's decision of 2026-09-14, recorded under `## Decisions`. It runs after Section 0 closes and before Section 1, because every later section adds tests and builds against the bar this section applies. The section number is its decomposition, not its build order.

The repository carries two test lanes, measured 2026-09-14 on SCOTT-CLAUDE at `6d382c7`.

The offline lane is ten suites holding 807 checks, and it runs in about 80 seconds. `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs` holds 499 of those checks and runs in 11 seconds. `.kit/supervisor-natural-exit-test.sh` is the slowest at 50 seconds, because it drives the real supervisor through a stub child.

The live lane is `.kit/live-all.sh`, whose `ALL_SUITES` names 17 suites. Each launches real `claude` children, and the last full run took 56 minutes. Two more live suites, `.kit/live-supervisor-test.sh` and `.kit/live-self-review-test.sh`, are tracked but never run by the gate.

So the gate's cost is the live suites rather than the check count.

Fix: every suite answers one question, and the answer decides whether it stays.
1. **A live suite** names what it proves that no offline suite proves. A suite with no such answer is retired. A suite whose proof can move to a stub-driven offline case moves there, and leaves the gate once that case is green.
2. **An offline check** pins a failure the code can actually produce, which a hard requirement depends on. A check that pins only a setting's value, a preference, or a configuration choice is removed.
3. **A duplicate** is removed wherever two cases prove the same thing.
4. **The two ungated live suites** are either registered with a reason or retired.

Files in scope: every `.kit/*-test.sh` and `.kit/*-test.mjs`, `.kit/live-all.sh`, `.kit/live-common.sh` (folded: the one place the live lane's model default lives), `.kit/assert-decisions.js` (the per-suite switch arms), `.kit/tick-harness.mjs` (only where a moved case needs a stub the harness lacks), the tracked fixtures `.kit/check-cap-reached.mjs`, `.kit/write-decisions-log.mjs` and `.kit/health-probe.js`, `.kit/.gitignore` (the tracked-suite roster), `README.md` (the Test Coverage catalog).

Acceptance:
1. Every suite left in `.kit/live-all.sh` states in its header what it proves that no offline suite proves.
2. Every retired or moved suite and every removed check is named in the Chapter with what still proves the behavior, or with the statement that nothing does and why that is acceptable.
3. No offline check remains whose only subject is a setting's value, a preference, or a configuration choice.
4. No two remaining cases prove the same thing.
5. The offline lane's and the whole gate's wall times are measured before and after on the same machine, and the Chapter carries both.

Tests: this section removes tests, so its gate is that every remaining suite passes. A new case is written only where a live proof moves to an offline stub, and that case is watched red before green.

## Decisions

### Every test earns its place, and a test audit runs before Section 1 - decided 2026-09-14 by the operator

The operator set a bar for tests on the Discord thread during Section 0's finishing pass. A test must be genuinely necessary, pinning something essential to functionality that is a hard requirement. Tests that pin simple preferences or intentional configuration are not wanted, the example given being a test that a menu has three items.

A second message asked for a task to look over every test against that bar and decommission the superfluous and preferential ones. The ground was a sibling project whose thousands of tests make every gate take over an hour.

The bar is in the Standing Brief Amendments block, so it rides on every section's dispatch from here on. Section 13 carries the audit. The dev session placed it before Section 1 because every later section adds tests and should build against the bar rather than be cleaned up after it, and told the operator the placement is theirs to move.

### Inbox upkeep is part of v2, before Section 3 - decided 2026-09-13 by the operator

The operator affirmed the commons-store inbox as the coordinator's transport. The concept had already worked for earlier prototypes and for the shared discussion file. The concern was its upkeep: whether messages are read, whether they are handled, how replies tie to requests, what shares the store, whether it grows forever, and how finished records are cleaned up.

Read against the code, the inbox answers read, reply linkage, sharing and growth. It has no handled state, its TTL sweep deletes unread records and skips the log, and its reply stamp can be taken by another turn. Three options were put to the operator: add a section now, build Sections 1 to 8 first and fix after, or fix only the cleanup now. The operator chose the first. The coordinator's core job is knowing what each worker finished, and without a handled state it would infer that from reply text, which is how a loop builds work nobody asked for. Section 12 carries the work.

### The idle anchor keeps updating on an unmatched turn completion - decided 2026-09-13 by a consult

Section 9 was written to stop an unmatched `turn.complete` corrupting two fields. Its own text left one of the two open, asking whether `lastTurnComplete` should update anyway on the reasoning that some turn did complete, while its Tests line pre-answered the same question the other way. A consult ruled it, and the answer is that the field is left exactly as it is.

Three readings decide it. `lastTurnComplete` has exactly one reader, the idle gate, which sits inside the controller tick whose second statement is `if (turnIsOpen()) return;`, so the gate is only ever read when no turn is open. The handler writes the field before it deletes the turn from the open-turn map, so a write made while a turn was still open is always superseded by the matched completion that finally empties the map. And the field is written twice more, on session start and on the ownership path, both times when no turn completed at all, which is not how a per-turn record behaves.

So the harm Section 9 names for this field, an unmatched completion restarting the idle clock while a turn is still running, is unreachable on this code. Conditioning the write would be a regression rather than a tightening: on a session whose starts go undelivered, the anchor would freeze while real turns kept finishing, the idle reading would climb without bound, and the gate would pass on every tick. That is the nudge pile-up Section 11 exists to prevent, re-entered through another door.

The axis that decides it generalizes to the next field in this handler, and it is not session-scope versus turn-scope. It is whether a wrong value is superseded in process before any reader sees it, or published out of the process where nothing overwrites it. `lastTurnComplete` is the first. `sess.turnStartedAt` is the second, published to the heartbeat by `writeOwnerHeartbeat` and read by another session on the deferred-status path, which is why it is the field this section changes.


Deriving the stamp buys a trade rather than a free win, and both review lenses named it, so it is recorded as accepted rather than left implied. Before this change the stamp was wrong during an overlap but self-healing, because any completion at all nulled it. Now it is only ever as honest as the open-turn map, and that map takes no age-out by the ruling recorded below. So a `turn.start` whose id never receives a completion pins the stamp for the life of the owner, and every reader then reports every pending record as deferred behind a turn that ended long ago, with the heartbeat staying fresh so a reader's dead-owner check cannot catch it.

That is accepted on the same ground the age-out refusal rests on: the map is in-process, so the only failure that can strand an entry is one that takes the process down and the map with it. What makes it worth naming anyway is that this section widens the consequence of being wrong about that. The check that would settle it is cheap and is filed in `docs/backlog.md`: log the turn id on both events, then read a live log and see whether every delivered start later completes. Until someone does, the no-strand claim is inferred rather than confirmed.

One consequence for both turn handlers. The stamp is written through a single `deriveTurnStartedAt` helper rather than by each handler stamping its own clock, because a start that stamped its own clock moved the published value forward whenever a second turn opened, and a reader watched one record's deferral shrink and then grow again when that second turn ended.
That also rules out the obvious shape for the fix. Skipping the clear when a completion is unmatched would let the stamp strand set forever whenever a start arrives whose own completion never does, and every reader would then report each pending record as deferred behind a turn that ended hours ago. The stamp is therefore derived from the open-turn map rather than conditionally cleared: after the delete it names the earliest turn still open, or null when none is.

### The open-turn map takes no age-out - decided 2026-09-13 by a scope ruling

Section 11 fixes the nudge pile-up with an in-flight reading keyed by turn id. Three fix rounds also tried to bound that map, so a turn id whose completion never arrived could not mute the goal loop forever. Each design reopened the defect it sat beside: keying the age on turn start evicted long legitimate turns, and refreshing every entry on any tool call meant a leaked id rode on a later turn and never aged out at all.

The second of those is two consecutive rounds of fix-introduced findings in one mechanism, which is a design stop rather than a third patch. The ruling refused the mechanism on three grounds. The Goal asks for nothing of the kind. No Out of Scope entry reaches it either, so the refusal rests on absence of an ask rather than on an exclusion. And the two acceptance bullets naming the bound were written from the code after the code existed, which ratifies a mechanism by construction and makes the question unaskable, so they carry no authority and were removed with it.

The correctness reading points the same way without deciding it. The harness guarantees `turn.complete` whatever the turn’s reason, including an abort, so a completion is lost only below the harness, such as on host death. The map is a plain in-process Map with no persistence, so host death takes it too. The bound therefore guarded a state the process cannot reach while repeatedly creating one it could.



### FORK B: coordinator steer authority - decided 2026-09-12, Option 2, autonomy with judgment

**The question.** Once Section 4 ships, a worker can tell a coordinator's record apart from anyone else's. What still needed settling was the rule for what that label is allowed to do: when a worker receives a `[COORDINATOR]` steer that would change files inside its own approved plan, does it act on it directly, or hold for the operator's confirmation first? Two positions were on record from two live sessions working this exact repo (the worker's Round 101, the Reviewer's Round 103), and the design council's authority lens (Round 1) took a position between them rather than picking one outright.

**The ruling, the operator's own words in substance (Round 107):** agree wholeheartedly with the coordinator having the ability to steer the worker. The premise is autonomy, abstracting as many decisions away from the operator as possible. It is a fine line, easy to land on the wrong side of - a sibling project (`ai-os`) did, running 30-plus review-round loops building features nobody wanted - but largely the coordinator determines what stays aligned with the goals and product vision and directs that on its own, and what is divergent or problematic and needs a conversation to realign or decide. It will not get this right every time, and that is acceptable; the closer it gets, the better the output.

**What this settles, concretely:**
1. **Option 2 is the rule.** A `[COORDINATOR]` steer inside a worker's approved plan is acted on without an operator round trip. Option 1 ("unverified until the operator confirms") is retired for coordinator records specifically; it still holds for any record that is not machine-labelled `[COORDINATOR]` (a plain reader, the Reviewer's own steers), which is exactly why Section 4's label and Round 106's subagent fix are prerequisites, not plumbing - Option 2 is only ever as safe as the label that triggers it.
2. **The bound is judgment plus a closed list of ask-first items, not a per-message confirmation and not a hard block either.** The operator ruled on this list directly (Reviewer thread, 2026-09-12, quoted in full): *"Are the exclusions hard blocks it can never do? Or are they things it will ask for approval on? If they're hard blocks, no, I don't want to make those things impossible. That said, I agree with all of them being things to ask me about first, except for three."* This settles the list's own nature: it is never a capability the coordinator structurally cannot exercise, only a class of act the coordinator surfaces to the operator rather than takes on its own delegated authority - the operator's own instruction, given directly, always reaches all of it. Four items stay ask-first: no push beyond the plan's own recorded commit model, no deploy, no settings/CLAUDE.md edits, no write outside the plan's own scope. Three items the Reviewer originally proposed (Round 103) are struck, in the operator's own words: *"1. I'm fine with onward dispatch on message content. 2. I'm fine with shared tier memory promotion at any time. 3. I'm fine with skipping the heavy process claim, it's just there to prevent slowness more generally, it can be bypassed when needed."* Onward dispatch on message content and shared-tier memory-lesson promotion are therefore in-grant, not ask-first; skipping the heavy-process claim protocol is likewise in-grant, since the operator's own reasoning names it a slowness guard rather than a safety boundary. The worker ties every steer to a goal node in its own tree under an approved plan and keeps the resulting act within that node's scope, same as before; the four remaining ask-first items and the operator's own aligned-with-goals-versus-divergent line are what routes a steer to the operator rather than acting on it directly. A wrong call lands on a branch and waits at merge: `agent_persona`'s branch protection (ruleset `protect-main`, id `22911862`) is confirmed active with `pull_request` required and `autoMergeAllowed: true` (Reviewer, Round 105, `gh` GraphQL read at 16:52Z) - a confirmed fact this Decision rests on, not a reported claim any longer, and no Open Question remains about it below.
3. **The escalation criterion belongs in the coordinator's own instructions, not the worker's, with a stated number rather than a deferred one (Reviewer Round 109 R12).** "Divergent and problematic enough to need a conversation" is the coordinator's own call to raise with the operator, not a rule the worker enforces on incoming steers. The guard against the named failure mode (the `ai-os` loop of many rounds building unwanted features) is a round cap of **two rounds per steer** - matching the cap the operator has already set on the `aios` worker today, rather than a number invented for this spec - plus a scope check against the plan's own Goal, both already in this kit's vocabulary (goal-tree round caps; the scope-adjudicator seat), rather than a human gate on every message. The coordinator's own operating instructions (Section 8, not Section 7 - Section 7 is the worker-side half, Reviewer Round 111 R19) state this plainly: *"If a steer would take more than two rounds to land, or the worker's own reading of it drifts from the plan's stated Goal, stop and raise it with the operator instead of pushing a third round."* Section 8 is where this actually gets built, not deferred further.
4. **The rollout order (Reviewer Round 109 R13, corrected per Round 111 R31): `ai-os` first, `Neuro-Evolution-Operations` not onboarded until the operator calls the `ai-os` run a success.** `NEO` is not a persona in this repository - it names `D:\Neuro-Evolution-Operations`, the operator's critical product repository and the coordinator's intended second worker repo. v2's coordinator mechanism is proven live against the `ai-os` repo's workers as a chronological experiment before the `Neuro-Evolution-Operations` repo gets a worker of its own under the coordinator; a future session executing this plan does not onboard it early on the strength of `ai-os` alone still being in flight.
5. **What stays open.** The kit's standing-grant rail rewrite (in flight as of this spec's authoring) is no longer a precondition for building Option 2 - the operator's ruling is the authority, not the rail - but it stays an alignment check: once the rewrite lands, re-read `skills/role/SKILL.md` and confirm the delegation chain it defines is still compatible with what this Decision states, and reconcile if not.

This Decision is recorded as the operator's ruling on the Reviewer's own thread (Round 107), overruling the worker's Round 101 position for coordinator-labelled records specifically. The worker's position stood as one live view weighed, not dismissed; it is recorded as considered and overruled by the operator, not by the Reviewer.

**Revised 2026-09-14, the operator's own ruling on the dev session's Discord thread:** the four ask-first items are struck. In the operator's words in substance: there is no reason to block any of those four actions inside the execution; anything that genuinely must be impossible is an external control (this repository's `protect-main` ruleset requires a pull request with one approval and refuses a direct push, and pull requests the session writes cannot be approved by the session), and everything up to that gate is allowed if the supervisor and the executing framework need it. A coordinator steer inside a worker's approved plan therefore acts on any of the four (a push, a deploy, a settings or CLAUDE.md edit, a change to the plan's commit model) on its own delegated authority, with the branch protection and the pull-request review as the gate. Item 2 above stands as the history of the list; this paragraph is the rule. One external control the ruling assumes is absent at revision time: the `ai-os` repository carries no ruleset and no classic protection (`gh api repos/SApplefeld/ai-os/rulesets` returns an empty list, the classic read returns 404, both read 2026-09-14). The operator accepts that gap for now (same thread, 2026-09-14): `ai-os` is a test prototype deployed nowhere, and the enforced controls go on it before it is used.

## Out of Scope

- Cross-machine coordination (the commons store is one machine-global file; a second machine is a different problem).
- A UI or dashboard for the coordinator's view of N workers; `agentic_inbox`/`goal_status`/the commons store are the interface for now.
- Automatic lesson promotion to a shared kit memory tier; the council named this as needing at least the same external-anchor bar item 8.2 already enforces plus a review gate, and it is deliberately left to a future section once the coordinator itself has run for a while and has real cross-repo lessons to promote.
- Retiring `DISCUSSION.md` as a physical file (only as a steering path going forward); its history stays as the record of v1's own build.
- Any change to the Discord relay, the broker, or `discord-channels` - the coordinator does not touch that surface at all in this design.

## Assumptions

- assumed 2026-09-12 (route b, low-blast default): the coordinator's own persona name is `coordinator` unless the operator prefers another; reversal costs a rename in Section 6's launch recipe and nothing else.
- assumed 2026-09-12 (route b, low-blast default; Reviewer Round 113 R38 - previously undeclared): the arming key (Section 6) defaults to `off`, so every plugin-loaded session that does not explicitly set `reader` or `owner` gets no persona tools and no claims at all; reversal is a one-line default change in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, affecting every session on this machine that omits the key.
- assumed 2026-09-12 (route b, this session's own authored pick, offered rather than sourced): the commons-store inbox path, not the harness's peer tools or Discord threads, is the coordinator-to-worker transport. A design council was convened and confirmed this pick against the plugin's own code rather than inventing it, but the pick itself is this spec's, not an existing source's ruling; reversal is a different spec, not a patch, since the rejected mechanisms were rejected on trust-boundary grounds this design relies on throughout. Flagged under Open Questions below in case the operator wants to weigh in before execution starts.
- FORK B (steer authority) was asked as route (c), material, decided by the operator 2026-09-12 (Round 107); recorded under `## Decisions` above rather than here, since it is now a ruling and not an open assumption.

## Open Questions

- Whether the kit's standing-grant rail rewrite (in flight as of this spec's authoring) keeps a Coordinator-to-Worker chain compatible with the Decisions section's FORK B ruling - owner: re-check `skills/role/SKILL.md` once the rewrite lands; the ruling itself does not wait on this, per the operator's own word (Round 107), but the two should be reconciled if they diverge.
- What label a coordinator gets on records *it* sends to the operator (this spec only decided the worker-facing labels) - low-blast, decide at execution time, likely no label at all since the coordinator reports via its own Discord thread, not this inbox path.
- The exact `tool.call` event field (if any) that distinguishes a subagent's own call from the top-level session's, needed by Section 4's urgent-break-in fix - owner: implementation time, checked against the real `ToolCallEvent` type, not assumed by this spec.

**On the HEAD this ran at.** `3407e93` is the tip of `item0-3-followup-runtime-clone`, not `main`. `main` was already `4e2115f` when the run started: that merge landed at 08:28:18Z, thirty minutes before the run began, not during it. The two commits are content-identical, with `git diff 3407e93 4e2115f` returning empty, so this is evidence for `main` as it stood, carrying Section 0's fixes 1, 2 and 3, and the two hook commits the item also names as needing this gate, `742015b` and `adfffcc`, both ancestors of `3407e93`. `3407e93` sits three documentation-only commits above `0fc66d2`.

The worktree also carried two uncommitted files, `.kit/tick-harness.mjs` and `.kit/controller-tick-test.mjs`, from unrelated kaizen work. No live suite loads either: `live-all.sh` runs the `live-*-test.sh` scripts named in its own `ALL_SUITES` and sources `live-common.sh`, and a search across `.kit` for both filenames, scoped to `*.sh` and `*.mjs`, matches only the files themselves.

**The one failure, `restartpassive`, is the defect `docs/backlog.md` already records,** in the test's observable rather than in the code. F1 through F4 passed. F5 greps the supervisor log for a `NOTE:.*backfilled` line emitted only on the natural-exit path, which this leg never reaches, because its backfill child sits on stdin until cleanup stops it.

That the mechanism itself works is carried over from item 3's own runs rather than observed here: this run retained only the seven check lines for that suite, with no store, supervisor log or decision log to read. Item 3 ran this suite four times, and the sequence matters more than a count. One run failed F1 and F2 before the R112 fix landed (`agent_persona_passive-supervisor_v1.md:613`). One failed them again after it, on planner nondeterminism rather than on the priming split (`:627`). The two after that ran green on F1 through F4 with only F5 and F6 failing (`:633`, `:653`). This run is the third consecutive one of that shape, and the word consecutive starts after the two F1/F2 failures rather than covering them. Only two of those runs left logs under `.kit/scratch/item3/`, so the plan doc lines above are the record for the rest.

**Contention, and a precondition that was not met.** Item 4 conditions this run on the operator confirming every other live `claude` process is stopped. That confirmation was never given and the condition was not established. Five `claude` processes were live when the box was polled just before the run, both supervisors and their children among them. That count is a session observation taken at that moment; the run directory records no process count, so nothing in the artifacts corroborates it or establishes that it held for the whole run. The operator was asked whether to run anyway and said to, on the belief the machine was quiet. It was not, and the reply saying so named only the foreign `.NET` suites rather than the `claude` processes. The gate additionally waited 1160 seconds for a foreign `.NET` test run in a third repository to clear, started on three consecutive clean samples, and a second foreign `.NET` run began after it was already going.

Sixteen suites passed clean through that, so the contention does not appear to have bitten. The honest statement is still that this run was not taken under the condition the item sets, and `restartpassive` deserves one re-run on a genuinely quiet box before its result is treated as settled. That caveat is about certainty rather than about the finding, which the backlog entry explains independently. The re-run is carried as its own `docs/backlog.md` entry rather than left in this prose, so item 4 is closed on evidence that names its own unmet precondition instead of on a green a future session would have to re-derive.

**A collection gap, new and routed to the backlog.** `stopprocesstree` reports `assert=[missing]` in the summary, the only suite of seventeen to do so. That is not missing coverage. The suite's own retained log, `.kit/runs/20260913T085812Z/stopprocesstree.stdout.log`, ends `15 checks run, 0 failed` and `live-stopprocesstree-test.sh: PASS`. The suite prints its checks to stdout and never writes the `<suite>.assert.log` file that `live-all.sh:132` collects, so the summary drops them. The gate's real evidence for item 2's process-tree stop exists and is simply absent from the summary line.

Gate: `.kit/live-all.sh`, all 17 suites, 16 `script_exit=0` and 1 `script_exit=1`. The exit code was read from a marker written by the wrapper that launched the run, `.kit/scratch/item3/live-all.exit`, holding `1`. `live-all.sh` itself writes no run-level marker, and its per-suite `script_exit` fields corroborate the verdict.

Commit model: Branch-and-PR, on `item0-4-whole-gate` off `4e2115f`.

Next: Section 0 item 5, the runtime switch from `--plugin-dir` to the installed marketplace plugin, whose own live check is the acceptance this item's evidence deliberately does not cover.

### Chapter 1 - 2026-09-13
Completed: 10. `goal_add` activates the node it creates when nothing else is active
Implemented By: implementer-sonnet, three dispatches (the section, then two fix rounds); the Minor close pass ran in the main session
Metrics: review rounds 3, closed claim-exit; provenance 6 spec-traceable, 4 fix-introduced, 0 new-requirement, rulings (0 refused, 0 declared, 0 asked); NEEDS_CONTEXT 0; escalations 0; consults 0
Decisions / Surprises: Section 10 asked for two decisions to be recorded here, and execution produced a third.

A childless `plan` node activates directly rather than waiting for a first child task. The ground is the tree's own leaf test, `!hasChildren`, which a node created moments earlier satisfies. Worth knowing beside it: activating a childless plan suppresses the planner's decomposition of it, because `isPlanningDue` returns false once any descendant is active. That is what the tick's own `activateNext` would have done too, so it is not a behavior change.

The spec's preference for calling `activateNext` was reversed, and this is a deviation from the section text, which has been updated to match. `activateNext` returns the oldest eligible leaf, and the node just created always has the newest timestamp, so calling it handed activation to some other node. Round 1's reviewer traced the consequence: `goal_done` acts on whatever is active, so the worker's next call completed a node nobody had worked on and credited it a round, while the node just added stayed pending. A silent wrong completion is worse than the loud refusal it replaced.

The third decision is the one that took two fix rounds to find. `activateNext` is not only an ordering rule. It is the single place deciding which nodes may be active at all: pending status, leaf, and an all-pending ancestor chain. Round 2 bypassed it and lost all three, which is why four of round 2's five Majors were one defect wearing four faces. The rule is now exported from `hooks/agent-state.ts` as `isActivationEligible`, `activateNext` reads it for its own leaf test, and `goal_add` asks it before activating. One definition, two callers.

Two surfaces were folded into this section rather than routed out. `hooks/agent-state.ts` was folded for the extraction above; it sits in the same directory as a file the section already changed, needs no acceptance the section does not carry, and the section's own gate covers it. Parent resolution in `goal_add` was folded because the section made a pre-existing branch reachable: a plan added with no `parentId` now resolves to the root whatever is active, since otherwise the first plan added in a turn became active and the second was denied for not being under the root.

`docs/backlog.md`'s entry for this defect is retired in this changeset, which is what the section existed to do.

A round 3 implementer reported that injected `[OPERATOR, urgent]` text had reached it in a tool result, instructing it to open a pull request, kill processes by pid and rewrite briefing conventions, and that it had refused. No injection occurred. Every occurrence of that marker in its transcript is this plan document's own Section 4 prose describing the Round 106 incident, which the agent read while reading the spec, plus its own report sentence. The lesson is banked in the operator memory tier.
Assumptions: assumed 2026-09-13 (route b, low-blast default, section 10): the controller's hold on the tree is exactly an open operator question and a nudge-cap pause. A node paused any other way does not hold the branch, because an operator can pause one node while another keeps working and a plan switch leaves a paused node behind that only a worker resume clears. Reversal is one clause in one condition.
Review Findings: review: code pair at opus, Workflow (round 1); code pair plus security at opus, Workflow (round 2); adversarial alone at sonnet, Workflow (round 3). The security lens ran in rounds 1 and 2, on the hook's allow and deny surface.

Round 1: one Critical, `activateNext` activating the wrong node, fixed. Four Majors, all fixed: the test could not detect the wrong node; `activate` was called with a null id that reset the nudge budget; the branch had no open-ask precondition where the tick's equivalent path has one; the branch could leave two nodes active at once through the nudge-cap restore path.

Round 2: no Critical. Five Majors, four fixed: activation into a closed subtree, which `completeLeaf`'s cascade then flips from abandoned to complete; a tree-wide paused guard that silently re-disabled the fix for a whole session; an open-ask case whose held node was also paused, so either clause could have refused it; a second same-turn plan add denied for not being under the root.

One Major justified-not-fixed: activation here ignores `sortKey`, so a node an operator reprioritized loses to the node the worker just created. Honoring it would reinstate round 1's Critical, the section's own title settles the conflict, and the operator's node wins the very next activation after `goal_done`.

Round 3: APPROVED, no Critical and no Major. The reviewer established the `activateNext` refactor behavior-preserving both by induction over the DFS's per-level status filter and by a full suite run.

Minors: 6 fixed in the close pass (the `check` helper now prints its third argument, which four call sites were already passing; a deny assertion implied by the one above it dropped; a `fireTick` second argument the function does not take; two unguarded node dereferences; the planning-gate control's root reseeded pending, since the root is never active in v3); 0 upgraded; 5 left with the reason (the active-node rather than active-leaf predicate, which only `goal_resume` can reach and where activating a second node would break the single-active invariant; the activation ordered before `await persist($)`, which is the shape the two branches above it already have; the yield path's untrue "this write was not saved" text, pre-existing; an orphan task resolving to the root, pre-existing and unchanged in outcome, only in timing; the same `fireTick` arity at two pre-existing sites outside this section's files).

One Minor from round 3 was fixed rather than held: `isActivationEligible`'s ancestor walk is now bounded by the node count and reads a cycle as not eligible rather than spinning.
Stamps: adjudicated 9, stamped 5. Applied: the splice-verified-by-diffing record, which caught a script that threw before its write so two edits never landed; the release-the-claim-at-the-operation-end record, which set the claim discipline across five gate runs; the kaizen standing grant, under which a note was written to the kit inbox; the queued-agent-looks-never-started record, which held the first-turn reading when two of three reviewers had started; and the forward-resource-arrangements record, under which every dispatch carried the box-budget clause. Four skipped as not acted on this stretch.
Gate: targeted lane at section close, on the branch tip with a clean worktree, with the supervisor fleet live on the box (11 node and claude processes at the first run). `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `node .kit/check-loader-rule.mjs` exit 0; `node .kit/commons-unit-test.mjs` exit 0; `node .kit/self-review-unit-test.mjs` exit 0; `node .kit/controller-tick-test.mjs` exit 0 with 398 checks and 0 failures. ### Chapter 3 - 2026-09-13
Completed: 9. An unmatched turn completion still corrupts the idle reading and the deferred-status stamp
Implemented By: implementer-opus, one dispatch; three fix rounds ran in the main session, the last of them a removal ordered by a scope ruling
Metrics: review rounds 3, closed major-closed; provenance 4 spec-traceable, 3 fix-introduced, 0 new-requirement, rulings (1 refused, 0 declared, 0 asked); NEEDS_CONTEXT 0; escalations 0; consults 1
Decisions / Surprises: this section ran after 10 and 11 rather than before them, which is ordinary. A plan's section numbers are its decomposition, not its build order, and the discriminating read is a Chapter's own Completed line.

Section 11 had already closed one of the three defects this section was written to fix. It moved the prompt text, the nudged-turn flag and the escalation count to the synchronous side of the submit, which is what the third defect asked for. Nothing was left to do and this section does not touch that code.

The section's own premise for the first defect was wrong, and a consult ruled it before any code changed. `lastTurnComplete` has one reader, the idle gate, which sits inside a controller tick that returns while any turn is open, and the handler writes the field before it removes the turn from the open-turn map. So a write made during another turn is always superseded before a reader sees it. Freezing the field, which the section's own Tests line asked for, would have let the idle reading climb without bound on a session whose starts go undelivered, which is the nudge pile-up Section 11 exists to prevent. The field is left exactly as it is and the ruling is recorded under Decisions.

The axis that ruling turns on generalizes: whether a wrong value is superseded in process before any reader sees it, or published out of the process where nothing overwrites it. That is why `sess.turnStartedAt` is the field this section changes and the idle anchor is not.
Assumptions: assumed 2026-09-13 (route b, low-blast default, section 9): the long-turn record's label falls back to the word unknown when a completion carries no usable turn id, rather than printing the word undefined into operator-facing text. Reversal is one expression.
Review Findings: review: code pair at fable, Agent tool (round 1); adversarial alone at opus, Workflow (rounds 2 and 3). The security lens did not run: the delta composes no permission grant, emits no allow or deny decision, and handles no untrusted input. It writes a timestamp a sibling session reads, which is a correctness boundary the code pair covers. Recorded as a decision rather than an omission.

Round 1, three Majors, all fixed: turn.start wrote the published stamp from its own clock while turn.complete derived it from the map, so opening a second turn moved it; the long-turn record skipped every completion with no map entry, which is most of them; and deriving the stamp makes it only as honest as a map that takes no age-out. The third was accepted rather than fixed and the trade is recorded under Decisions with the cheap check that would settle it.

Round 2, three Majors, all fixed: the long-turn test case omitted the duration field so it exercised a fallback rather than the branch production takes; preferring the event field removed an accidental duplicate suppression; and the record's label came from a counter that only counts starts this session saw.

Round 3, APPROVED_WITH_CONCERNS, one low-confidence Major and five Minors.

Design stop. Rounds 2 and 3 each returned a fix-introduced Major in one mechanism, the long-turn record's duration source, which is the provenance trigger rather than a severity one. It was noticed at round 3's adjudication after that round's Major had already been fixed, which is out of order and is recorded as the deviation it is. The judge returned refuse and ordered the block cut back.

That ruling was checked rather than adopted on its face, because its grounds rested on acceptance bullets this session composed for the dispatch and the plan carried none. The check the memory record prescribes is to grep the plan for the mechanism, and it came back harsher than the ruling: the plan never asked for the duration field, the comparison between two figures, or the duplicate suppression. The comparison and the suppression were removed, along with the cases pinning them, because both defended against failures nobody has measured inside the one block already producing self-inflicted defects.

The duration field itself was kept and is declared drift. A record depending on the map alone is blind on a session where most starts are never delivered, which a reviewer established and which serves the Goal. Section 9 now carries an Acceptance list so the next judge rules against the plan rather than against a reconstruction.

One dispatch of the scope adjudicator failed at its fable tier on an API safeguard flag, an environment fault rather than a review outcome. The one same-model retry ran and ruled.
Stamps: adjudicated 5, stamped 3. Applied: the plan-section-number record, which settled that running 9 after 11 is ordinary; the optional-field distinct-count record, which caught a dedupe keyed on an event id that would have silently suppressed every later long turn had the id ever been absent; and the line-endings record, which is why every ending check here read raw bytes and which caught 52 bare-LF lines this session put into a CRLF test file. Two skipped as peer-session reads rather than this session's.

A child that exited on its own aborted the supervisor. Bash unsets `CHILD_PID` and the `CHILD` array when it reaps a coproc, and the script runs under `set -u`, so the natural-exit branch had never completed on any run. Every read of the child's pid now uses one pid saved at launch, and a stub-driven suite covers the branch no live suite can reach. The three RESTART_PASSIVE restarts the plan credited to that branch came through the decide path's stop.

The stale bound reached a `node` program by splicing, and an implementer's red run executed an injected payload through it. Both programs in the pre-launch gate now take the persona and the bound through argv. The commons half had also held a fixed 90000 and ignored its own parameter.

Two design stops fired. The shared numeric check carried fix-introduced Majors in rounds 2 and 3, and the pin proving every setting is checked carried them in rounds 3 and 4. The scope adjudicator ruled accept-and-declare on both, grounded on the amendment bullet naming the shared check. Its non-blocking observation stands as an open quality question: `supervisorPsBoundS` falls back to 30 silently where every other setting exits with an error line.

The operator set a bar for tests mid-pass: a test must pin a failure a hard requirement depends on, never a setting or a preference. It was applied at adjudication. Six Minors asking for more pin machinery were left with their reasons, and one case added in this pass was removed as a duplicate of an existing one. The operator then asked for an audit of every suite against the bar, which is appended as Section 13 and recorded under Decisions, with the bar in the Standing Brief Amendments block. A D3 extension (two more ticks after the cap, classify stays 2) stayed green under the step-4 break `if (!activeNode || activeNode.status !== "active")` to `if (!activeNode)`, because the nudge-cap check returns before the classifier. A standalone case seeding a paused leaf also stayed green under that break: `enforceInvariants` nulls `activeGoalId` for a paused node on parse, and every in-session pause is followed by an earlier return or nulls the id itself, so no single break reaches the classifier from a paused leaf. Under that same break `caseS13_errorStreak` reddens (`probe-pausedtree.summary.txt`: the paused node fell to the idle gate and no ask_waiting was logged), so the guard is pinned by that case. Recorded rather than shipped as a check that cannot go red.

### Interim board 6 - 2026-09-14
Section 1 (Commons entry carries turn state and workdir) is between review rounds. Implementer-opus built it from `.kit/scratch/s1/brief.md`; the first-green state is `e07f6cd`. Round 1 ran the code pair at fable, low, Agent tool: adversarial returned 0 Critical, 0 Major, 3 Minor; blind returned 1 Major (a session yielding ownership mid-turn stranded a non-null `turnStartedAt` in its commons entry, because the turn.complete stamp was owner-gated and the reader tick preserves the field) and 4 Minors. The Major is orchestrator-traced to the section's own sentence that the fields track a turn's lifecycle the way the heartbeat stamp does, spec-traceable, and is fixed in `0e3edf0` by an unconditional stamp in both turn handlers with a harness case that drives the yield through the heartbeat tick's persona-store check. That commit also covers the live `e.cwd` branch of the workdir read and corrects two comments. Both commits are pushed. The Minor file is `.kit/scratch/agent_persona_coordinator_v2/minors-section-1.md`; two Minors stay open for the close pass or a reason (the reader tick's `claimReaderRole` passing no meta in `hooks/operator.ts`, out of scope; `workdir` never refreshed after a cwd change, `CwdChanged` being a classic settings-hook input rather than an `on` event).

### Chapter 6 - 2026-09-14
Completed: 1. Commons entry carries turn state and workdir
Implemented By: implementer-opus (three dispatches: the section, the round 1 fixes, the round 2 fixes); the Minor pass rode the round 2 dispatch
Metrics: review rounds 2, closed major-closed; provenance 2 spec-traceable, 0 fix-introduced, 0 new-requirement, rulings (0 refused, 0 declared, 0 asked); NEEDS_CONTEXT count 0; escalations 0; consults 0
Decisions / Surprises: The commons-entry stamp at `turn.start` and `turn.complete` is written whatever the session's role, while the heartbeat file keeps its owner-only write, because a session that yields mid-turn finishes the turn as a reader and an owner-gated stamp would strand the non-null value (round 1's Major). The round 1 fix brief mis-stated the yield path as the heartbeat tick's commons arbitration at `hooks/index.ts:1173`; that site is `shouldYield` over the persona store, and the implementer drove the yield by rewriting `.agentic-personas.json` instead, which the case now does. Round 2 found the reader-role write sites (`claimReaderRole` at `hooks/index.ts:1090`, `:1192`, `:3338`, through `hooks/operator.ts`) still passed no meta, so a reader's entry published `workdir ""` until its first turn and a gc-recreated entry reset the stamp mid-turn; round 1 had recorded the same surface as a Minor left out of scope, and round 2's failure scenario upgraded it. `hooks/operator.ts` was folded into the section (same directory, no new acceptance, the tick suite covers it), an approval-drift widening of the section's Files in scope line, made above. The round 2 fix's predicted red did not reproduce as briefed: `createTickHarness` fires `session.start` at creation as the owner, which already writes the workdir, so the new case drops the creation-time entry before re-firing as a reader (`joinAsReader`, which `seedReaderHarness` now calls before its own overwrite). The fake store's `get` now returns a structured clone, since the map-aliased object let a mutation land before `set` ran; with `store.set` removed from `stampCommonsMeta` the suite went to 4 failures, so the clone bites. `releaseReaderRole` gained the meta parameters for symmetry and has no caller in `hooks/index.ts`. ### Chapter 8 - 2026-09-14
Completed: 12. Inbox upkeep: a handled state, cleanup that never loses unread work, and a reply link no other turn can take
Implemented By: implementer-opus (the first dispatch through the round 4 fix, then a fresh dispatch for the round 5 fix through the close pass); the round 5 note bound and one comment in the close pass by the main session
Metrics: review rounds 8, closed major-closed; provenance 8 spec-traceable, 8 fix-introduced, 3 new-requirement, rulings (1 refused, 2 declared, 2 asked); NEEDS_CONTEXT count 0; escalations 0; consults 1; review-round backstop fired once (after round 5, operator continue bought three rounds, the section closed on the third)
Decisions / Surprises: The section shipped `agentic_resolve` (owner-gated in its handler, since Section 6's tier does not exist yet; refuses pending, skipped, already-resolved, another persona's record, a bad outcome and a note over 2000 characters), a TTL sweep that never deletes pending work and appends every record it removes to `.agentic-channel.jsonl` before deleting, a window roll that keeps delivered and answered records until resolved, and a reply link built on an in-memory list of the plugin's own queued turns. The link took most of the rounds. The spec's phrase 'any other turn the plugin did not open for the record' named no mechanism, and each fix answered the reviewer's reading of that phrase and then failed the next one: two booleans (round 2), an ordered list resting on submission order (round 3), a match on the submitted text (round 4), a second key on the text the submit settled to plus the drop arm (round 5), a store-keyed exit for dead entries (round 6), a copy taken before that exit's read (round 7), and removing a pending-keep clause that guarded nothing (round 8). Section 6 (coordinator launch shape, config key and the arming tiers) is at its approach-confirmation read, done, with the brief written at `.kit/scratch/s6/brief.md` and not yet dispatched. Section 5 closed at Chapter 11 (`f099d11`, pushed); the tree is clean there.
Design fixed in the brief, from the code read this session: one key `arming` with values `off`, `reader` and `owner`, absent or unrecognized reading as `off` with a `ui.log` line naming the value. `off` registers one `session.start` hook that logs and returns, so no tools, no timers, no claims and no store writes, and peer text reaches the model as the harness delivers it. `reader` registers `agentic_identity`, `agentic_say` and `agentic_inbox` only, never the controller tick, and can never own: `session.start` takes the reader branch whatever the store holds, the heartbeat promotion never runs, `agentic_identity` takes only a reader claim, and `prompt.submit` appends no context block (the `[NO GOAL]` reminder at `hooks/index.ts:4655-4667` fires unconditionally today, which is the nag the section names). `owner` is unchanged. `emit_settings_json` always writes `arming` `owner` and `coordinatorPersona` (from `COORDINATOR_PERSONA`, default `coordinator`, refused when `default`) and exports the variable. Two folds, approval drift to record in Chapter 12: `bin/supervise.sh` gains one call to a new `ensure_settings_arming`, because this machine's own `run/settings.json` predates the key and a relaunch would otherwise start the dev worker `off`; `.kit/live-budget-test.sh` hand-writes settings for a direct `--plugin-dir` launch and gains the key. Intake gaps routed: the key name and the `off` semantics are route (b); the default is the plan's own Assumptions entry; the reader tool set is Section 12's text; the reader-never-owns rule is what makes the section's no-backfill and no-nudge clauses structural, and it closes the defect the project record `killing-the-owner-child-hands-the-persona-to-the-reviewer` describes. The live launch acceptance (a Fable coordinator process with its own Discord thread) is an outward act that takes the operator's yes; it is best taken at the finishing pass once Sections 7 and 8 give the coordinator its instructions, and the section notice asks that.

The ruling's remedy: replace the commit-model arm with a snapshot of the recorded model taken at coordinator delivery that later deliveries may only tighten and only an operator-origin prompt resets; re-anchor the origin on the set of delivered, unresolved coordinator record ids plus the origin kind at submit, dropping `lastTurnWasCoordinatorOrigin`. Its operator fork: accept the wider bound (every non-operator turn between delivery and resolution) or keep matched turns only with the fail-open continuations as residual. The ruling is adopted as the recommendation in the declaration and nothing is built on it until the operator answers.
